Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7491: Side (secondary) churches and chapels must also have their own iconostases; and holy tables, as long as they do not have their own iconostases, are considered unsuitable for divine services.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7515: ##### 13. Prayers with extended and uplifted hands are recited only when this is explicitly prescribed, that is: at the prayer "No one is worthy" (at the Cherubic Hymn), at "Let us lift up our hearts" and at the Lord's Prayer. When extending the hands, the elbows should not rest on the sides, but the hands themselves should be freely lifted upwards.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7522: a) The side doors always remain closed, unless someone enters or leaves through them.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7533: ##### 22. When putting on and kissing vestments, it is necessary to follow the prescriptions given at the beginning of the Proskomedia (see nos. 103-106); at other divine services, having left aside the corresponding prayers, one must observe the same.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7550: ##### 33. When the Choir begins to sing "Lord, I have cried," the Deacon takes the censer and, after the Priest blesses the incense, censes the holy table from the four sides, then the icon behind the altar, the icons of the sanctuary and the Priest[^758]. After this, having gone out of the sanctuary through the northern doors[^772], he comes before the holy doors, makes a bow, censes the icons of the southern part of the iconostasis, beginning from the icon of the Savior; then, having made again a bow before the holy doors, censes the icons of the northern part, beginning from the icon of the Theotokos, then - the choirs, right and left; He censes the people from the solea, after which He returns into the sanctuary through the southern doors.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7551: ##### 34. When the Choir begins to sing the last sticheron, the Priest puts on the phelonion, having blessed and kissed it, and, at "Glory," comes with the Deacon before the holy table. The holy doors are opened. The Deacon takes the censer and, after the Priest blesses the incense, together with him, who now lowers the phelonion, goes around the holy table, beginning from the right side and, preceded by candle-bearers, they go out through the northern doors. They stand before the holy doors: candle-bearers on opposite sides, one - near the icon of the Theotokos, the second - near the icon of the Savior, the Deacon - in the middle, and behind him - the Priest. The Priest and deacon bow their heads. The Deacon, holding the orarion with three fingers, says quietly: "Let us pray to the Lord," and the Priest recites quietly the Prayer of the Entrance "In the evening and in the morning and at noonday."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7552: On the Eve of the Nativity of Christ and Theophany, if they fall on a Saturday or Sunday, the Deacon takes for the Entrance, besides the censer, also the Gospel.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7555: The Priest blesses to the east and says: "Blessed is the entrance." Then the Deacon presents to the Priest the Holy Gospel for kissing, if he has it, and after the Choir finishes the stichera, stands in the holy doors before the Priest, makes the sign of the cross with the censer or lifts the Gospel up[^759], if he has it, and exclaims: "Wisdom, arise." During the singing of "O Gladsome Light," the Deacon and priest enter the sanctuary before the holy table, and the candle-bearers return there through the side doors. Then the Deacon censes everything, as during "Lord, I have cried" (see no. 33).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7588: ##### 39. When the Choir begins to sing "Lord, I have cried," the deacons take censers and, having put in incense, approach the Priest. The Priest blesses the incense and both deacons cense: the throne - first from the front, then the 1st deacon - from the right side, and the 2nd - from the left, and again together - from the east, then - the icon behind the altar, other icons of the sanctuary and the Priest. After this, they go out of the sanctuary, the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern, come before the holy doors and, having made a bow, cense the icons of the iconostasis, the 1st deacon begins from the icon of the Savior, and the 2nd - from the icon of the Theotokos, then the 1st deacon - the right choir, and the 2nd - the left. They cense the people from the solea, the 1st - those on the right, and the 2nd - those on the left side. Then they return into the sanctuary, the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern doors.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7605: ##### 46. For the Entrance, he takes the censer or the Holy Gospel, goes around the holy table from the right side, exits through the northern doors, bows his head before the holy doors and recites the Prayer of the Entrance "In the evening and in the morning"; with his right hand he blesses the entrance, kisses the Gospel, if he has it, and then exclaims: "Wisdom, arise," making the sign of the cross with the censer or lifting the Holy Gospel with both hands, and returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary. During the singing of "O Gladsome Light," he censes, as was noted (see no. 33), returns into the sanctuary and stands before the throne.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7606: ##### 47. After the hymn "O Gladsome Light," he approaches the holy doors and, looking at the people, says: "Let us be attentive" and blesses the people: "Peace be to all" and adds: "Wisdom, let us be attentive." Then, if there be readings, he turns to the throne, goes from the right side behind the throne and exclaims before the title of each paremia: "Wisdom," and after the title: "Let us be attentive."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7610: ##### 50. Concelebrating priests, whose number should be even, put on before the Entrance only epitrachelia and phelons over their ryasas and stand on the sides of the holy table, and not before it, where only the principal celebrant and deacons stand; they stand according to the order of their dignity or ordination: the first priest - on the right side of the throne, the second - on the left, the third - again on the right, and so on.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7612: After the call of the Deacon "Wisdom, arise," all enter immediately after the Deacon through the holy doors and take their places on the sides of the holy table. During the censing of the holy table by the Deacon, the concelebrating priests move away slightly from it.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7620: Then the Priest takes the censer, blesses it and censes the holy table from the four sides, then - the icon behind the altar and other icons of the sanctuary, the table of oblation, the icons of the iconostasis, first - of the right, and then - of the left part, the choirs and the people, as prescribed. During the censing, the Deacon goes before the Priest with a candle and, having returned before the holy doors, says quietly: "Bless, Master" (see nos. 33, 34, 39).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7628: At the end, the Priest exclaims: "Hear us, O God" and blesses the people: "Peace be to all." The Deacon exclaims: "Bow your heads," the Priest with a raised voice recites the prayer "O Master, Great in mercy" and, after its conclusion, all return into the temple to the tetrapod, on which are prepared for blessing five loaves, wheat, wine and oil; the Priest and deacon stand before it, and the candle-bearers - to the right and left of them on opposite sides.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7629: During the singing of the Aposticha Troparion, the Deacon, after the blessing of the incense, censes the tetrapod from the four sides and only the Rector and the Priest.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7655: ##### 63. After the exclamation "Blessed and glorified be," the Priest goes out with the cross in his hand, and the deacons - with censers through the northern doors or, if according to custom the holy doors are open, the Priest - through the holy (see no. 57), and the deacons - through their doors. From here they proceed to the narthex or, if there is none, to a place near the entrance to the temple, preceded by the Choir and candle-bearers. The deacons, after the blessing of the incense by the Priest, cense, both from their side, the holy icons, if they be in the narthex, the Priest, the Choir and the present people. After the completion of the Litiya stichera, the deacons sing alternately the prayers:
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7671: ##### 64. During the singing of the Aposticha Troparion, the deacons cense the tetrapod from the four sides: first both - from the front, then the 1st deacon - from the right side, the 2nd deacon - from the left, then both go behind the tetrapod and cense it from the east, and after this - only the Rector and the Priest.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7680: ##### 67. The Priest in epitrachelion and phelonion opens the holy doors, censes the holy table from four sides and everything else, as noted (see no. 54). Turning to the holy doors he says in a half-voice: "Lord, bless."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7683: ##### 69. After the exclamation "Blessed and glorified be," the Priest goes out with the cross in his hands through the northern or, if there exists a custom, holy doors. From here he goes to the very narthex or to a place near the entrance doors. The Choir and candle-bearers also gather in the narthex. The Priest takes the censer, censes the icons, if they be in the narthex, the Choir and the people. After the completion of the Litiya stichera he himself reads the litanies. After the completion of this, he returns to the temple, approaches the tetrapod, censes it from four sides and after this - only the Rector.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7687: ##### 71. Then, during the procession at the Litiya, all make a small bow before the holy table and go out, in front - the younger ones, whether through the northern, or, if there exists a custom, through the holy doors, having lowered their phelons and hands down. In the narthex or at the place near the church doors they stand in two rows, one beside the other, and the principal celebrant - in the middle, behind all. The Deacon or deacons stand near the principal celebrant.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7688: If only one deacon censes, then, having censed the icons in the narthex, he censes first the principal celebrant, then - the concelebrants on the right, and finally those standing to the left of the principal celebrant. If both deacons cense, each censes those standing on his side. If there are no deacons, the principal celebrant censes the concelebrants, beginning from those standing on his right hand.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7702: "Lord, have mercy" (12), "Glory, Both now," "Come, let us worship" (3). The Priest enters the sanctuary through the southern doors, recites the Prayer of the Censer, takes the censer, censes the throne from the four sides, the icon behind the altar and other icons of the sanctuary. Then he goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors, comes before the holy doors and, bowing, censes the icons of the southern part of the iconostasis, beginning from the image of the Savior, the icons of the northern part, beginning from the icon of the Theotokos, then - the choirs, right and left. He censes the people either from the solea or crossing the whole temple, while the Choir reads the 20th(19th) Psalm "The Lord hear Thee" and the 21st(20th) "The King shall joy." When the Choir begins the Trisagion, the Priest returns before the holy doors, censes them and after "Our Father" exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom," enters the sanctuary through the southern doors, stands before the holy table and, after the completion of the troparia "O Lord, save Thy people," exclaims the Litany for Emperors. After the exclamation "For a merciful,"
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7719: The Priest exclaims: "For Holy Art Thou, our God." The Deacon censes the holy table from the four sides and everything else, according to custom (see no. 33).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7740: ##### 80. If there was a Vigil, the Priest after the exclamation "By the grace," holding the cross in his hands, goes out through the holy [doors], and the Deacon with the oil, blessed at Vespers, and the anointing brush, - through the northern doors and they stand on the right side of the tetrapod. There the Priest anoints crosswise the forehead of everyone who approaches to kiss the Holy Gospel. After the completion of the anointing [Myrovanne] they return to the sanctuary: the Priest - through the holy, and the Deacon - through the southern doors and, having closed the holy doors, the Priest takes off the phelonion.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7742: After the singing of the Katavasia of the eighth Ode, the Deacon takes the censer and, having stood before the icon of the Theotokos, says: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light let us magnify in songs" and, having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, censes the holy table from four sides and everything else, according to the prescription (see no. 33).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7782: ##### 89. A Priest who serves Matins without a Deacon does and says everything that belongs to the Deacon, considering this:
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7792: ##### 94. Concelebrants, in an even number, put on epitrachelia over their ryasas and put on phelons during the reading of the Kathisma and stand on the sides of the holy table, as at Vespers (see no. 50). During the singing of the Polyeleos, all go out, having made a small bow, through the holy doors, having lowered the phelonion and hands down, in front - the younger ones. Two first ones carry the icon of the feast or saint, if it has not yet been placed on the left side of the tetrapod between both choirs, and place it on the tetrapod; all stand on the sides of the tetrapod in two rows, facing one another, the principal celebrant stands in the middle before the tetrapod, and to his right - the Deacon.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7793: ##### 95. Then all sing the Magnification. When the Choir repeats the Magnification, the Priest, preceded by the Deacon with a lighted candle, censes from four sides the icon of the feast or saint on the tetrapod, the holy table, the sanctuary, the iconostasis, the concelebrants, both choirs, the people and once more the icon of the feast, but only from the front. If, however, the usual censing takes place before the Gospel, then at the Magnification the Priest censes only the icon itself.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7825: ##### 97. According to the ancient custom of the Eastern Church, only one Holy Liturgy may be celebrated at the same holy table. However, one may depart from this rule if the Divine Liturgy must be repeated for the benefit of the faithful. If there is one or several secondary churches in the parish, then the more solemn Liturgy is celebrated in the main sanctuary, and other liturgies should be celebrated rather in the side sanctuaries.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7849: After this he goes to the table of oblation, unfolds the antimension [iliton], places the diskos on the left side, and the chalice - on the right, and prepares also the spear, star-cover, veils and purificators for the holy offering of the sacrifice.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7857: After this the Priest takes a prosphora with his left hand, and with his right - the holy spear, makes with it the sign of the cross three times over the seal of the prosphora and says thrice: "In remembrance of our Lord and God," immediately thrusts the spear into the right side of the seal, cuts and says: "As a sheep led to the slaughter," and into the left: "And as a blameless lamb," into the upper side of the seal: "In His humiliation," into the lower: "And Who shall declare His generation." And the Deacon, observing piously this mystery, says each time: "Let us pray to the Lord," holding the orarion with His right hand. Then He says: "Take up, Master."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7858: The Priest, having thrust the holy spear obliquely into the right side of the prosphora, lifts out the holy bread and says: "For His life is taken away from the earth" and, having placed it face up on the holy diskos, after the call of the
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7860: cuts it crosswise and says: "Sacrificed is the Lamb of God" and turns the other side of the bread, which has the cross, upwards. The Deacon says: "Pierce, Master." The Priest pierces the holy bread with the spear in the upper right corner, where the letters "IC" are imprinted, saying: "One of the soldiers."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7893: ##### 117. After the dismissal of the Proskomedia, the Priest goes before the holy table, and the Deacon censes the table of oblation three times, then the holy table from the four sides, saying quietly: "In the tomb" and the 50th(51st) Psalm, after which he censes the icon behind the altar. He goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors, comes before the holy doors and, having bowed before them, censes the icons of the southern part of the iconostasis, beginning from the icon of the Savior, then - the icons of the northern part, beginning from the icon of the Theotokos, and then - the choirs, right and left. He censes the faithful either from the solea, or, if there is a custom, going through the church to the very narthex. Having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, he censes the holy table once more, only from the front, and likewise - the celebrant (see nos. 9, 20, 33, 39, 54; also no. 177).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7924: ##### 122. The Third Antiphon or the Beatitudes, if it is Sunday, is sung. When the singers reach "Glory," the Priest and deacon, standing before the holy table, make three small bows. Then the Priest takes the Holy Gospel, gives it to the Deacon and lowers the phelonion. Both go around the holy table from the right side and, having gone out through the northern doors, preceded by candle-bearers, make the Little Entrance. Having come before the holy doors, the candle-bearers stand on opposite sides: one - near the icon of the Theotokos, and the other - of the Savior, the Priest - in the middle, and the Deacon - slightly in front of him, to the right. Both bow their heads and the Deacon says in a low voice: "Let us pray to the Lord," and the Priest recites quietly the Prayer of the Entrance "O Master, Lord our God."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7938: It is known that the Priest should not go up to the High Place, nor sit on it. It befits him to sit on the southern side of the High Throne.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7951: The Deacon, having given away the censer, approaches the Priest and, having bowed his head before him and holding the orarion with his fingers, points to the Holy Gospel and says loudly: "Bless, Master, the proclaimer of the holy Apostle." The Priest, blessing him, exclaims: "May God, through the prayers," and gives Him the Holy Gospel, and the Deacon answers: "Amen" and, having bowed to the Holy Gospel, takes it, goes out through the holy doors, preceded by candle-bearers who go out through the side doors, stands on the ambo or not far from the holy doors at the designated place, there places the Holy Gospel on the analogion and reads it, turned to the people.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:7964: The Deacon comes to the very holy doors and gives the rolled Holy Gospel to the Priest[^771], who kisses it and places it in the middle of the throne before the tabernacle or places it on the right side of the holy table; the holy doors are closed again.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8000: After this, where there exists a custom, the Priest and deacon kiss the holy table and, if they wish, the altar cross; then they go, the Deacon - in front, directly to the side altar. There the Priest, having taken the censer, censes the Holy Gifts crosswise, praying quietly: "O God, be merciful to me a sinner," gives the censer to the Deacon, who holds it on one of the fingers of his right hand.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8004: Then he takes the holy diskos, covered with the small veil, and places it with attentiveness and piety on the Deacon's head[^774], and the Deacon, holding the end of the orarion with his left hand, holds the diskos with both hands on his head. The Priest himself takes the chalice, covered with the small veil, and holds it before him. Both go out through the northern doors, preceded by candle-bearers. These last arrive at the very holy doors and stand on both sides of them.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8005: The Deacon, going out, calls: "All of you, Orthodox Christians" and enters through the holy doors into the sanctuary, where he waits for the Priest on the right side.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8044: Then the Deacon approaches the Priest from the left side and, having taken the holy star-cover with his right hand, strikes the holy diskos with it above, below, on the left and on the right side, making thus the sign of the holy cross over it, and, having kissed the star-cover, places it on the holy table.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8100: and the Priest blesses it saying: "Blessed is the fervor of Thy saints." The Deacon pours a little water in the sign of the cross into the holy chalice, saying: "The fervor of faith" and, having set aside the vessel with the warm water, stands slightly at a distance[^778].
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8101: ##### 140. The Deacon goes around the holy table and, if necessary, washes the palm of his right hand and, at the words of the Priest "Deacon, approach," approaches him from the left side, bows to him, asking forgiveness.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8157: ##### 149. After the dismissal of the Proskomedia, the deacons cense the offering with three swings, and then go together with the Priest before the holy table, saying quietly: "In the tomb with the body" and the rest, they cense together it from the front, then the 1st deacon - the right side of the holy table, and the 2nd deacon - the left, and again both together - from the east, then - the icon behind the altar and other icons of the sanctuary, as well as the 1st deacon - the cross, and the 2nd deacon - the icon of the Theotokos, if they be behind the throne (see no. 33). Then they go out of the sanctuary: the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern doors. Having come before the holy doors, they cense the icons of the iconostasis: the 1st deacon begins from the icon of the Savior, and the 2nd - from the icon of the Theotokos, after this the 1st deacon - the right, and the 2nd - the left choirs. They cense the faithful, one from the right, and the other from the left side whether from the solea, or, if there exists a custom, - in the church itself, going to the very narthex, after which they enter the sanctuary: the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern doors and cense once more the throne from the front with three swings and likewise the Priest.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8163: ##### 152. When the singers, who are singing the third Antiphon or the Beatitudes, reach "Glory" or the last verses of the Beatitudes, the Priest and deacons make three small bows. Then the Priest hands the Holy Gospel to the 1st deacon, and himself lowers the phelonion (see no. 34). The Priest with the 1st deacon go around the holy table from the right side, during which the 2nd Deacon takes the censer, and all go out through the northern doors, preceded by candle-bearers: first goes, censing, the 2nd deacon, then - the 1st deacon, carrying the Holy Gospel, and behind them - the Priest. Having approached the holy doors, the candle-bearers stand on opposite sides: one - near the icon of the Theotokos, and the other - near the icon of the Savior, the 1st deacon - on the right, and the 2nd - on the left side, and the Priest stands in the middle behind them.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8185: ##### 155. During the singing of "Alleluia," the deacons, having taken censers and put in incense, approach the Priest and say: "Bless, Master, the incense" and, having received the blessing, cense the holy table first from the front, then the 1st deacon - from the right side, and the 2nd - from the left and again both together - from the east, then - the icon behind the altar and other icons of the sanctuary, the 2nd deacon also - the oblation, and both - everything else, according to custom.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8209: After the censing, the deacons stand on the sides of the Priest, holding the censers on the ring finger of the left hand and together with the Priest, lifting their hands up, recite all together three times the Cherubic Hymn, after each time making a small bow (see no. 129).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8215: The deacons, having entered through the holy doors into the sanctuary, stand not far from them on opposite sides: the 2nd - on the left, and the 1st - on the right. After the words "All of you" the Priest enters the sanctuary and, having approached the front with the deacons, places the holy chalice on the throne, also takes from the 1st deacon the holy diskos and places it on the throne to the left of the chalice; with the large veil, which he takes from the 2nd deacon and censes over the censer, he covers the Holy Gifts, then takes from the hands of the 2nd deacon the censer and censes with it the Holy Gifts (see no. 130).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8224: The deacons, having made a small bow, enter through their doors into the sanctuary and stand on the sides of the Priest. The 1st deacon, if necessary, takes the fan or the small folded veil and fans over the Holy Gifts.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8225: At "Singing the triumphant hymn," the 2nd Deacon takes with his right hand the star-cover and strikes with it on the holy diskos, as was said above about this and, having kissed it, lays it aside on the throne (see no. 133).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8230: After the completion of the prayer, the 1st deacon lays aside the fan or veil and both deacons approach the Priest, who together with them makes three small bows. Then the 1st deacon, pointing with the orarion to the Holy Bread: "Bless, Master, the Holy Bread." And the Priest, bowing, blesses the Holy Bread with the words: "And make this Bread."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8240: ##### 160. During the singing of "It is truly meet," the deacons cense the throne from the sides, each - from his own, and from the east and commemorate the deceased, whom they wish.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8257: and the Priest blesses it saying: "Blessed is the fervor of Thy saints." The Deacon pours a little water in the sign of the cross into the holy chalice, saying: "The fervor of faith" and, having set aside the vessel with the warm water, stands slightly at a distance[^778].
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8258: ##### 163. The Deacon goes around the holy table and, if necessary, washes the palm of his right hand and, at the words of the Priest "Deacon, approach," approaches him from the left side, bows to him, asking forgiveness.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8312: ##### 170. A Priest who serves without a Deacon performs and recites all that which belongs to the Deacon, considering the following:
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8316: ##### 174. For the Little Entrance, the Priest takes the Holy Gospel, goes around the throne from the right side and goes out through the northern doors. Bowing his head before the holy doors, he recites the Prayer of the Entrance "O Master, Lord our God," blesses the entrance with His right hand, kisses the Gospel and, showing it, exclaims: "Wisdom, arise." After this He enters the sanctuary through the holy doors (see no. 122, note).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8317: He finishes the Prayer of the Trisagion with the exclamation "For Holy Art Thou," not turning to the people. After the Trisagion, He goes behind the throne, reciting the words of blessing, and sits on the southern side.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8333: ##### 177. Having given away the censer, he exclaims the litany "Let us complete," "Help us, save us, have mercy." Reciting the Symbol of Faith he lifts the aer and holds it over the Holy Gifts. According to the prescription, exclaiming "Singing the triumphant hymn," he strikes the diskos with the star-cover as the Deacon does, kisses it and lays it aside on the holy table (see no. 133).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8343: ##### 185. The Proskomedia should be celebrated not in the sacristy, but on the side altar [table of oblation] which is located near the holy table. The custom of using five prosphora is not necessary, on the contrary, it is permitted to use only one prosphora at several liturgies, even if the sign of the Lamb on the prescribed side is not imprinted, however, at every Liturgy the Priest is obliged to cut the bread and take out particles as if the bread were whole, not using dried lambs and not using either the Lamb or particles cut earlier by the one who serves.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8357: ##### 197. All concelebrants, while putting on the prescribed priestly vestments, recite the appropriate prayers. Then each, holding in his hands a small *Liturgicon*, stands at the side of the holy table or also behind it, however not before it, where only the principal celebrant with the deacons occupies the place, and so all arrange themselves, each according to the rank of his dignity or his ordination. The first among the concelebrants or those standing by stands on the right side of the principal celebrant, the second - on the left, the third - again on the right and so on.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8375: ##### 201. The concelebrants sit near the High Throne in the same order in which they stand at the throne, that is, those who stand to the right of the principal celebrant sit to his right, and those on the left side - sit to the left.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8380: ##### 206. When the principal priest kisses the Holy Gifts, the concelebrants one by one approach the holy table, according to the order of their dignity. The principal priest, having stepped back slightly from the holy table, turns to the right side and looks to the north, and each concelebrant kisses first the Holy Gifts, and then the left and right shoulder of the principal priest and stands to his left, slightly behind. The second concelebrant kisses first the Holy Gifts, then - the principal celebrant, and at the end - the first concelebrant and stands on his right side. All others do likewise. The principal Priest says: "Christ is in our midst," and the concelebrant responds: "He is and shall be." Likewise do also the others, who kiss one another.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8385: ##### 209. After the breaking [of the Lamb], the principal celebrant calls the Deacon and gives him a particle of the Holy Bread, as prescribed. Then all recite the prayer "I believe, O Lord" and the principal priest communicates of the Body of Christ. All concelebrants, in turn, according to their dignity, approach the holy table from the side of the oblation table, and the principal celebrant stands on the opposite side of it. After the communion of the Body of Christ, each goes to the north of the holy table and washes, if necessary, His fingers in the vessel standing on the table of oblation, and, according to the prescribed sequence, they go behind the 1st concelebrant, who stands at his place at the holy table. The Deacon consumes the particle of Bread after the priests. The principal celebrant, having returned to the center, communicates of the Most Precious Blood and moves away from the holy table; after this the concelebrants, according to the prescribed order, approach the holy table, this time from the southern side, and each after the communion of the Blood departs by the table of oblation and stands at his place at the holy table. At the end the Deacon receives, according to the prescription, the communion of the Most Precious Blood from the principal celebrant.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8397: ##### 217. It is not required that all concelebrants go out for the Little Entrance, but if they go out, then the one who is the 1st among them carries the Gospel, he himself recites the Prayer of the Entrance and imparts the blessing. But it is always required that those standing before the holy table or on its right side, before they go out, go around the holy table and go out behind the holy doors.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8402: ##### 221. On the Holy and Great Forty Days [Lent] at the Proskomedia of the Divine Liturgy on Sunday or on the last day before the Liturgy of Presanctified Gifts, besides the Lamb that is to be consecrated on that day, other Lambs are also prepared for those Liturgies of Presanctified Gifts that are to be celebrated on the following days. Having cut, sacrificed and pierced the first Lamb, the Priest performs the same rites also over the other Lambs, reciting at each of them: "In remembrance," "As a sheep," "Sacrificed," "One of the soldiers" and places them one near another on the diskos. Then he pours wine and water into the holy chalice, having recited what is prescribed and, according to custom, finishes the Proskomedia.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8428: ##### 226. At the beginning of the reading of the psalms [stichologia], if the Presanctified Lamb is kept in the tabernacle (artophorion) of the holy table, the transfer to the table of oblation takes place thus[^785]: the Deacon brings to the holy table the holy diskos, the star-cover and the small veil. The Priest kisses the Holy Gospel and places it on the right side of the holy table. After this he places the diskos on the unfolded antimension [iliton], on its left side, and, having opened the tabernacle, makes a small bow. He takes the chalice with the lid, in which the Lamb is kept, places it on the middle of the antimension [iliton] and, having taken from it the Presanctified Bread, places it with great piety on the holy diskos, as also the particles needed for the communion of the faithful. Having placed the pyx back into the tabernacle, he closes it and, having placed the holy diskos on the middle of the antimension [iliton], censes the star-cover and the small veil and covers the diskos with it. Having taken the censer, he censes around the holy table, preceded by the Deacon with a lighted candle. Having returned before the holy table and given the censer to the Deacon, he makes a low (prostrate) bow, making the sign of the cross on himself, after which he takes with both hands the covered holy diskos and, holding it on his head crosses by the right side of the holy table to the table of oblation (altar of preparation), preceded by the Deacon with a lighted candle and censer.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8458: Then again the Reader or priest, who now, accompanied by the Deacon going in front, stands on the southern side of the holy table and censes there, sings the first verse of the 140th Psalm: "Lord, I have cried," and the Choir repeats: "Let my prayer be set forth."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8460: After this the Reader or priest, preceded by the Deacon, stands on the northern side of the holy table, censes there and sings the third verse of the psalm: "Incline not my heart," and the Choir sings: "Let my prayer be set forth."
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8464: ##### 228. The holy doors are closed and the Deacon, having received permission from the Priest, goes out through the northern doors and, having stood at the designated place, sings the litany "Let us all say." Everything else takes place according to the prescription until the very exclamation of the prayer for the catechumens, after which the Priest, having laid aside the Gospel, unfolds the antimension [iliton] on the holy table.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8472: ##### 228. They go out of the sanctuary through the northern doors, in front walks the Deacon, who frequently censes the Holy Gifts. During the procession no one says anything. When they enter through the holy doors into the sanctuary, the Priest places the holy diskos on the throne on the left, and the holy chalice - on the right side, removes the small veils from the diskos and chalice and places them on the holy table, and with the aer, which he takes from the shoulder of the Deacon and censes over the censer, covers the Holy Gifts, saying nothing, but only censing the Holy Gifts thrice. After the completion of the hymn "Now the Powers of Heaven" all make three great prostrations, after which the holy doors are closed and the curtain is drawn shut.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8475: Then the Priest removes the aer and, having folded it, places it aside, as also the star-cover. The Deacon enters through the southern doors into the sanctuary, stands to the right of the Priest and, having made a small bow, says: "Break, Master, the Holy Bread," and the Priest breaks it and places the four parts on the diskos just as it takes place at the Liturgy of Chrysostom, and the particle with the inscription "IC" he places into the holy chalice, saying nothing. The Deacon pours a little warm water into the chalice, if there exists a custom, saying nothing and stands somewhat to the side. The Choir sings the Communion Hymn "Taste and see," and also a second one, if the Apostle and Gospel of some saint or temple saint were read.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8566: ##### 224. At the Great Entrance candle-bearers, if there be such, turned to the northern doors and arranged in order, accompany the Presanctified Lamb. After they arrive before the holy doors they stand together on both sides, and then return to their places.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8846: [264] Ancient Greek typikons mention nothing about a deacon, neither at the bringing out, nor at the exaltation, nor at the laying of the precious Cross, but only about one priest. The Greek typikon, similarly to the Slavic ones, prescribes for the priest to vest in all priestly vestments: αλασσει ο Ιερεύς πασαν την ιερατικην στολην /the priest vests in all priestly vestments/. The Greek *Triodion* (Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross) and the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, similarly to Slavic typikons, prescribes for the priest to enter with the cross from the sanctuary to the temple through the northern doors: εκ του αριστερού Βήματος /from the left side of the sanctuary/. Greek typikons, with the exception of the new Constantinopolitan, and the Slavic of the Lviv University, prescribe for the priest, having exited from the sanctuary to the temple, to head to the entrance doors that lead from the temple to the narthex, which are sometimes called royal, and there await the end of the Doxology and from there exclaim "Wisdom, attend", and then head to the tetrapod, which is before the iconostasis. Although these doors are called royal, βασιλικαι /royal/, one must understand that they were called so in the past. Today we call royal the middle doors of the iconostasis, which according to ancient custom are called holy: αγιαι ΰυραι /holy doors/ (chapter 96, Greek Typikon).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8862: 97. According to the ancient custom of the Eastern Church, only one Holy Liturgy may be celebrated at the same holy table. However, one may depart from this rule if the Divine Liturgy must be repeated for the benefit of the faithful. If there is one or several secondary churches in the parish, then the more solemn Liturgy is celebrated in the main sanctuary, and other liturgies should be celebrated rather in the side sanctuaries.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:8880: [277] Some consider, according to the edition of the Peremyshl Typikon of 1870, p. 271, that neither on St. Michael, nor on St. Prophet Elias, should "Blessed is the man" be sung and, that then not a single kathisma is taken, for this kathisma, as the publisher writes about it, is found only in the Pochaiv *Anthologion*, and in the most ancient menaia and Anthologia they are not mentioned; the reason for this is considered to be that "Elias did not die, and St. Michael, as bodiless, is not subject to death". However, this is not enough. For regarding the rule, not only the Pochaiv *Anthologion* (*Anthologion*) of 1777 and the Pochaiv *Menaion* of the same year, but also the Lviv *Anthologion* (*Anthologion*) of 1643 and the Great Typikon, under the title Tserkovne Oko, printed in Moscow in 1615, that is more than a century and a half before the Pochaiv *Anthologion*, and the manuscript *Anthologion* of 1736, which we have, and the Typikon of the All-Russian Church, printed in Moscow in 1859, by the decision of its Governing Synod, give also on these two feasts the kathisma "Blessed is the man". Although some rules, like the Moscow *Menaion* of 1782, the Greek-Venetian of 1884 do not give "Blessed is the man", it does not follow from this that no kathisma is needed, for even on Saints without Polyeleos in the *Menaion* a kathisma is not given, but the sequential one is meant; so here one can understand the sequential one, which three ancient handwritten Slavic typikons give: one -- of the Lviv University, the second -- of St. Onuphrius in Lviv, and the third -- of the library of the Chapter of St. George; also Tserkovne Oko (which gives two rules: one with "Blessed is the man", and the second with the sequential kathisma). The reason that these rules give for these two feasts a sequential kathisma, may lie in the fact that their service initially had neither All-Night Vigil nor Polyeleos, but gradually, with time, grew into All-Night Vigil. About St. Elias it is written in the Pochaiv *Menaion*: "Let it be known that in printed Greek menaia, in handwritten typikons, and also in Slavic handwritten and printed typikons, the service of the Prophet Elias is given on 6, with *Octoechos*". And regarding St. Michael, the above-mentioned Slavic typikons give him a service only of the Polyeleos type, but incomplete, for they partially combine his service with the service of the *Octoechos*, as for a Saint without Polyeleos.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9218: [^17]: Some of us consider that here it is not proper to say "Lord, bless," but only "Bless," because here a blessing is not asked from the Lord, but from the Priest. But this we do not know for certain, for in other Typikons, namely in the Moscow Typikons and Horologia in the order of Vespers, and in the Greek Horologia at Daily Midnight Office, the words "Lord" are absent; however, in our Horologia (at the end of Matins) and in the Tserkovne Oko, and in the Greek Euchologia in the order of Vespers this word is given. Here are the words of the Roman Euchologion: "Ο ιερεύς ησυχως: Δόξα σοι ο θεός, η ελπις ημών, δόξα σοι; και λεγουσιν οι Αδελφοί: Δόξα, και νυν, Κύριε ευλογησον /The Priest quietly: 'Glory to Thee, O God, our hope, glory to Thee.' And the brethren say: 'Glory, Both now,' 'Lord, bless'/." Therefore, both the former and the latter are good, for it is possible to ask the Lord God for a blessing at the conclusion of the Divine Service, just as is asked at Vespers with Vigil, at the beginning of which the Deacon, or the Priest himself, exclaims: Κύριε ευλογησον /"Lord, bless"/.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9242: [^41]: Let it be known that besides the Great Forty Days there exist two other periods in which the rubric of the Menaion on September 21 prescribes also a 3rd Sessional Hymn with a Small Litany and a Kathisma before it. The first period lasts from the Apodosis of the Feast of the Holy Cross, which falls on September 21, to the Forefeast of the Nativity of Christ, which falls on December 20 exclusively. The second lasts from the Apodosis of the Feast of Theophany to Cheese-fare Sunday exclusively, that is in these two periods, in which at Sunday Matins the Polyeleos is given. However, in the content of our Psalter three Sessional Hymns and three Kathismata are given only in the Great Forty Days, so we use them.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9458: [^257]: Thus we consider; for in the case when the typikon does not give the Kontakion of the Forefeast, this one is added later.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9465: [^264]: Ancient Greek typikons mention nothing about a Deacon, neither at the bringing out, nor at the exaltation, nor at the laying of the precious Cross, but only about one priest. The Greek typikon, similarly to the Slavic ones, prescribes for the Priest to vest in all priestly vestments: αλασσει ο Ιερεύς πασαν την ιερατικην στολην /the Priest vests in all priestly vestments/. The Greek Triodion (Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross) and the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, similarly to Slavic typikons, prescribes for the Priest to enter with the cross from the sanctuary to the temple through the northern doors: εκ του αριστερού Βήματος /from the left side of the sanctuary/. Greek typikons, with the exception of the new Constantinopolitan, and the Slavic of the Lviv University, prescribe for the Priest, having exited from the sanctuary to the temple, to head to the entrance doors that lead from the temple to the narthex, which are sometimes called royal, and there await the end of the Doxology and from there exclaim "Wisdom, attend", and then head to the tetrapod, which is before the iconostasis. Although these doors are called royal, βασιλικαι /royal/, one must understand that they were called so in the past. Today we call royal the middle doors of the iconostasis, which according to ancient custom are called holy: αγιαι ΰυραι /holy doors/ (chapter 96, Greek Typikon).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9473: [^272]: Thus our Pochaiv Menaion, Tserkovne Oko and Moscow Typikon. But our liturgikons, horologia and the Greek-Venetian Menaion of 1755 prescribe to take the Service of the Holy Fathers on the Sunday following 11 October; one must understand, that when 11 October falls on a weekday; similarly in the latest Greek-Venetian Menaion (1884) and in the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, where it is prescribed to take on the very day of 11 October, if Sunday, if -- not, then on the following Sunday. What shall we say to this? We consider that all these rules are good, however, it is not good when liturgical books of one and the same church province give uncoordinated rules. For when we accept the rule of our Menaion, then let it be placed also in the liturgikons And in the horologia, for when we accept the rule of the latter, then let It be also in the Menaion. And if we adhere to the rule of the Menaion, then it is necessary to present it already on 8 October, so that, reading, one can notice about the Following Sunday. This must be understood also about the Service of the Holy Fathers of the First Six Councils on 16 July, which the menaia both ours and Greek, Prescribe to transfer back, to 13 July, if it falls on Wednesday; the Greek menaia present this not in accordance with their rule on 11 October, when they prescribe to transfer this service from 16 July back, they have to transfer it back also from 11 October. It seems to us, And it would be simpler not to transfer these services back, but to take on the very [days of] 11 October and 16 July, when they fall on Sunday, if -- not, on the following Sunday.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9478: [^277]: Some consider, according to the edition of the Peremyshl Typikon of 1870, p. 271, that neither on St. Michael, nor on St. Prophet Elias, should "Blessed is the man" be sung and, that then not a single kathisma is taken, for this kathisma, as the publisher writes about it, is found only in the Pochaiv Anthologion, and in the most ancient menaia and Anthologia they are not mentioned; the reason for this is considered to be that "Elias did not die, and St. Michael, as bodiless, is not subject to death". However, this is not enough. For regarding the rule, not only the Pochaiv Anthologion (Anthologion) of 1777 and the Pochaiv Menaion of the same year, but also the Lviv Anthologion (Anthologion) of 1643 and the Great Typikon, under the title Tserkovne Oko, printed in Moscow in 1615, that is more than a century and a half before the Pochaiv Anthologion, and the manuscript Anthologion of 1736, which we have, and the Typikon of the All-Russian Church, printed in Moscow in 1859, by the decision of its Governing Synod, give also on these two feasts the kathisma "Blessed is the man". Although some rules, like the Moscow Menaion of 1782, the Greek-Venetian of 1884 do not give "Blessed is the man", it does not follow from this that no kathisma is needed, for even on Saints without Polyeleos in the Menaion a kathisma is not given, but the sequential one is meant; so here one can understand the sequential one, which three ancient handwritten Slavic typikons give: one -- of the Lviv University, the second -- of St. Onuphrius in Lviv, and the third -- of the library of the Chapter of St. George; also Tserkovne Oko (which gives two rules: one with "Blessed is the man", and the second with the sequential kathisma). The reason that these rules give for these two feasts a sequential kathisma, may lie in the fact that their service initially had neither All-Night Vigil nor Polyeleos, but gradually, with time, grew into All-Night Vigil. About St. Elias it is written in the Pochaiv Menaion: "Let it be known that in printed Greek menaia, in handwritten typikons, and also in Slavic handwritten and printed typikons, the service of the Prophet Elias is given on 6, with Octoechos". And regarding St. Michael, the above-mentioned Slavic typikons give him a service only of the Polyeleos type, but incomplete, for they partially combine his service with the service of the Octoechos, as for a Saint without Polyeleos.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9502: [^301]: Thus the Pochaiv Menaion and Moscow Typikon. Regarding the troparia, our liturgikons correctly prescribe the Troparion of the Forefeast in the second place, for so they are taken on the Sunday of the Fathers, which is before the Descent of the Holy Spirit; however, there is no need to adhere to this, for the Troparion of the Fathers can be considered not of the Lord. Regarding the kontakia, our horologia do not have the Kontakion of the Fathers and give a separate Kontakion to the Forefeast, in which the Holy Fathers are mentioned.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9555: [^354]: Greek typikons order to sprinkle the temple, and our Euchologia -- also the Holy Table. Slavic menaia give the following rule about the sprinkling of monks: "And the Superior, having taken holy water in a bowl and turning his face to the west, holds with his left hand the precious Cross, and with his right -- basil, and sprinkles crosswise on all sides. Then the priests approach first, two by two, and kiss the precious Cross, and the Superior signs them with basil with holy water crosswise on the forehead; in the same way also all the brethren. All sing the troparion many times, until all the brethren are sanctified by the sprinkling with water and kissing". Similarly also the Constantinopolitan Patriarch signs with holy water the hierarchs and church dignitaries (Ορφικιουχους /officials/), as the new Constantinopolitan Typikon gives.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9607: [^406]: About the Katavasia in the odes where the three-ode canon does not come, the typikon is silent. Although according to the general rule of the Apodosis of feasts the Katavasia should be of the Feast, and after each ode, however, we think that the Katavasia of the Feast in this case does not have greater weight than in the Afterfeast, for the service of the Feast on this Apodosis has little. Finally we consider that the Katavasia in this case should be only after the 3rd, 6th, 8th and 9th odes, namely the heirmos of the last canon, that is after the 3rd, 8th and 9th -- the heirmos of the three-ode canon, and after the 6th -- of the Cross.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9611: [^410]: Since there are 4 kathismata, that is 3 sequential ones and Polyeleos, the question arises, which litanies should be sung here; for the general rule has only three. To this, since the feast has three sessional hymns, and here only two are given, then which two of the three to sing here? Regarding the litanies, our and Russian typikons mention litanies after the 3rd kathisma, from this it follows that after the 1st and 2nd kathismata there are no litanies, which the Pochaiv Triodion also excludes after the 1st kathisma before the sessional hymn of the Triodion on 25 March; and assuming such, it is proper to take two small litanies: one -- before the post-Polyeleos sessional hymn, with the exclamation "For blessed is Thy name", and the second -- before the sessional hymn of the 3rd kathisma, with the exclamation "For Thou Art good" or "For Thine is the dominion", at choice. But because only the Russian and Romanian typikons do not prescribe the Small Litany at Matins on a weekday, and with us this is not adhered to, therefore we think that here only after the 1st kathisma one need not take a litany, and after the 2nd one should take with the exclamation "For Thine is the dominion", after the 3rd -- with the exclamation "For Thou Art good". And of the two sessional hymns of the feast let one be taken, which is after the Polyeleos, and the second -- one of the first two, at choice.

Although according to the general rule of the Apodosis of feasts the Katavasia should be of the Feast, and after each ode, however, we think that the Katavasia of the Feast in this case does not have greater weight than in the Afterfeast, for the service of the Feast on this Apodosis has little. Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9757: [^556]: Besides the initial Kondakion "To the Directress", the rest of the Akathist consists of 12 Ikos and 12 Kontakia, together -- of 24 members according to the number of 24 letters of the Greek alphabet, with which each member, in the Greek text, begins in sequence.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9768: [^567]: The Pochaiv Molitvoslov of 1793 prescribes this dismissal "on Sunday evening, on Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday of HOLY and Great Week at Matins and at the Hours"; the Pochaiv Horologion of 1811 adds to Wednesday also Thursday at Matins. However, the rubrics of the Triodion at these Matins do not indicate a dismissal, for they are small and on the first three days -- of Lenten form and are combined with the 1st Hour. At the Hours one should not omit this dismissal, nor at the Presanctified, as we consider; Tserkovne Oko of 1615 and, before it, the Liturgikon of Ballaban of 1604 extend this dismissal to the whole Passion Week, except Saturday.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9820: [^619]: According to the new custom of the Greeks, which Slavs do not accept, before the beginning of Matins the Priest sings the Sunday Gospel of Matins, usually -- the 2nd. This the Greek Typikon of Sabbas does not have, but only newly published Greek Pentecostaria. Also according to the custom (and not according to the church rubric) of the Greeks, independent of the Patriarch of Constantinople, the entrance into the church is accompanied by questions and answers taken from the 23rd Psalm: "Lift up your gates, O ye princes, and the King of Glory shall come in" etc. and when is sung: "The Lord of Hosts, He is the King of Glory", the doors are opened and the entrance into the church takes place... "Especially the custom prevailed to enter through question and answer: Lift up gates, and to open the door only at The Lord of powers, He is the King of Glory /Especially the custom prevailed, that the entrance into the church be accompanied by dialogue, question and answer 'Lift up the gates', and the doors are opened only at 'The Lord of Hosts, He is the King of Glory'/, -- is written in the description from Corfu. This custom we meet also among Greek-Melkites, and Italians, and also in various churches of the Latin rite... However, neither in Greek nor in Slavonic rubrics is this met, nor the words of the named psalm fit well here, for according to general explanation they refer not to the descent of Christ into hell, but to His Ascension...
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9826: [^625]: Compare the rubric of the Sunday of Ap. Thomas in the evening with the rubric of Small Vespers of the Ascension of the Lord. About this also in the new Typikon of the Patriarchate of Constantinople is written: "Many also after the Resurrection of our Lord Jesus Christ, ignoring, invoke the O Heavenly King Comforter, etc. in which this is not pardoned, our Savior being still on earth, except from the 1st of the Ascension, or from the first Pentecost, because at the third hour of this day descended the Holy Spirit... as from this therefore the invocation of the Comforter Spirit took beginning, from this day, which ceases from the day of the Resurrection until Pentecost. (On Monday of the Holy Spirit) /Many not considering, also after the Resurrection of our Lord Jesus Christ, read 'O Heavenly King, Comforter' etc., which -- is inadmissible, when our Savior will still be on earth until the first hour of the Ascension, or until the first hour of Pentecost, for at the third hour of this day descended the Holy Spirit... as, finally, from this began to invoke the Holy Spirit the Comforter, from that day, which ceases from the day of the Resurrection until Pentecost (On Monday of the Holy Spirit)/.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9832: [^631]: If a Bishop serves, we consider that he should begin Vespers in the sanctuary, censing before the holy table and from there read the verses, after which depart to his throne. And at "O Gladsome Light", after the blessing with the di- and trikirion he enters the sanctuary and from the holy doors reads the Gospel, after which returns to his throne.
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9833: [^632]: Some consider (among them also the new Typikon of the Patriarchate of Constantinople) that throughout the whole Bright Week Vespers should begin not from "Blessed is our God", but from "Glory to the Holy", as Matins. However, they are mistaken, for in all editions which we have, is indicated clearly "Blessed is our God": "Blessed is our God"... and the Greek Typikon of Sabbas: "Signs crosswise with the censer, saying: 'Blessed is our God'" (On the Sunday of the Resurrection, at Vespers).
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9964: [^763]: These gifts are arranged according to such a scheme so that the blessing of the priest depicts upon them the sign of the cross: loaf • wine • • oil • wheat **Chunk 74** **IV. Rubric of Matins in Sundays and Feasts** **1. In Concelebration of One Deacon** **74.** If before this there was no Vigil, then Matins begins thus^[764]: **75.** The priest puts on the *ryasa* the epitrachelion, having previously blessed it, goes before the holy doors and begins: "Blessed is our God." Choir: "Amen," "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee," "O Heavenly King," Trisagion, "Most Holy Trinity," "Our Father." Priest: "For Thine is the kingdom." Choir: "Amen," "Lord, have mercy" (12), "Glory, Both now," "Come, let us worship" (3). The priest enters the sanctuary through the southern doors, recites the Prayer of the Censer, takes the censer, censes the throne from the four sides, the icon behind the altar and other icons of the sanctuary. Then he goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors, comes before the holy doors and, bowing, censes the icons of the southern part of the iconostasis, beginning from the image of the Savior, the icons of the northern part, beginning from the icon of the Theotokos, then -- the choirs, right and left. He censes the people either from the solea or crossing the whole temple, while the choir reads the 20th(19th) Psalm "The Lord hear thee" and the 21st(20th) "The King shall joy." When the choir begins the Trisagion, the priest returns before the holy doors, censes them and after "Our Father" exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom," enters the sanctuary through the southern doors, stands before the holy table and, after the completion of the troparia "O Lord, save Thy people," exclaims the Litany for Emperors. After the exclamation "For a merciful," Choir: "Amen," "In the name of the Lord, bless, father." The priest exclaims^[765]: "Glory to the Holy, Consubstantial." Choir: "Amen," "Glory to God in the highest" (3) with small bows and "O Lord, open my lips" (2). The Six Psalms are read. **76.** During the reading of the Six Psalms^[766] the priest, standing before the holy table, begins to recite the Matins Prayers. After the first three psalms, he goes out through the northern doors and, having stood before the holy doors, continues to read the Matins Prayers. After the completion of the psalms, the priest responds to the bow of the deacon and, having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, stands before the holy table. At the end of the Six Psalms, the deacon, dressed in the sticharion with the orarion, goes out through the northern doors, pays respect to the priest, stands on his usual place and exclaims the Great Litany. The priest exclaims: "For to Thee belongs." During the singing by the choir of "God is the Lord," the deacon recites the verses and, after a small bow, returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary. **77.** After the Troparia, the reader reads the regular Kathisma, after which the deacon [exclaims] before the holy doors -- the Small Litany, and the priest exclaims: "For Thine is the dominion." Then the reader reads the second regular Kathisma, the deacon -- the second Small Litany, and the priest -- the exclamation "For Thou art a good God." After the Troparia "The Angelic Council," if it is Sunday, or after the Magnification [*Velychannya*], if it is a feast, the deacon again reads the Small Litany, and the priest exclaims: "For blessed." **78.** After the Gradual [*Stepenna*], the holy doors are opened, and the deacon approaches the holy doors and, looking at the people, exclaims: "Let us be attentive." The priest, dressed in the phelonion, blesses the people: "Peace be to all." Deacon: "Wisdom, let us be attentive." The choir sings the Prokimenon. Deacon: "Let us pray to the Lord." Choir: "Lord, have mercy." The priest exclaims: "For Holy art Thou, our God." The deacon censes the holy table from the four sides and everything else, according to custom (see no. 33). Choir: "Let everything that has breath praise the Lord." The deacon recites both verses, and the choir repeats: "Let everything that has breath," after which the deacon: "And that we may be vouchsafed." Choir: "Lord, have mercy" (3). The priest takes the Gospel, goes out before the holy doors, stands at a slight distance before them on the solea, places the Gospel on the analogion and reads it, turned to the people. Deacon: "Wisdom, arise, let us hear the Holy Gospel," and the priest blesses: "Peace be to all." Choir: "And to thy spirit." Priest: "The reading from the Holy Gospel according to (Name)." Deacon: "Let us be attentive." The priest reads the Matins Gospel. **79.** After the reading, the priest kisses the closed book and carries it to the middle of the temple, where he places it on the tetrapod, and he himself returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary. The deacon reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," if it is Sunday, or the reader -- the 50th Psalm "Have mercy on me, O God." After the sticheron, the deacon [is] at his usual place: "O God, save Thy people." Choir: "Lord, have mercy" (12) and the priest exclaims: "By the grace and compassions." Choir: "Amen." The Canon begins. The holy doors, if there was no Vigil, are closed. **80.** If there was a Vigil, the priest after the exclamation "By the grace," holding the cross in his hands, goes out through the holy [doors], and the deacon with the oil, blessed at Vespers, and the anointing brush, -- through the northern doors and they stand on the right side of the tetrapod. There the priest anoints crosswise the forehead of everyone who approaches to kiss the Holy Gospel. After the completion of the anointing [*Myrovanne*] they return to the sanctuary: the priest -- through the holy, and the deacon -- through the southern doors and, having closed the holy doors, the priest takes off the phelonion. **81.** After the third and sixth Odes, the deacon sings the Small Litany either before the holy doors, or near the tetrapod, if the anointing was not yet finished, and the priest exclaims the exclamation either from the sanctuary, or from the tetrapod. After the singing of the Katavasia of the eighth Ode, the deacon takes the censer and, having stood before the icon of the Theotokos, says: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light let us magnify in songs" and, having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, censes the holy table from four sides and everything else, according to the prescription (see no. 33). After the ninth Ode and after the recitation by the choir of "It is truly meet," if it is not Sunday, but if it is Sunday or a feast, after the singing of the Heirmos, the deacon exclaims from his place the Small Litany. The priest exclaims: "For all the powers of heaven praise Thee." The choir sings the Exaposteilarion. **82.** After the Praises stichera, at "Glory," the deacon opens the holy doors. The priest in the phelonion stands before the throne, the deacon stands to his right and, after the completion of the last sticheron, the priest, turned to the east, exclaims: "Glory to Thee, who hast shown us the Light." The Great Doxology is sung. Then, also according to custom, the Troparion is recited, and the priest, preceded by the deacon, carries the Holy Gospel from the tetrapod into the sanctuary and places it on the holy table. **83.** After this, the deacon stands at his usual place and exclaims the litanies "Have mercy on us, O God" and "Let us complete." The priest blesses the people and exclaims: "Peace be to all." Choir: "And to thy spirit." Deacon: "Bow your heads unto the Lord." Choir: "To Thee, O Lord." The priest recites quietly the prayer "O Holy Lord," after which he exclaims: "For Thine it is." The dismissal follows, which the deacon begins: "Wisdom" (see no. 36). **84.** After the dismissal, the reader immediately begins the 1st Hour: "Come, let us worship" (3) and the psalms. After "Our Father," the priest in the epitrachelion: "For Thine is the kingdom." Reader -- Kontakion and "Lord, have mercy" (40). The priest goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors and before the holy [doors], turned to the east, loudly: "Thou who at all times". Reader: "Lord, have mercy" (3), "Glory, Both now", "More honorable than the Cherubim", "In the name of the Lord, bless, father." Priest: "God be merciful to us." Reader: "Amen." Priest recites the prayer "O Christ, the True Light", "Glory to Thee, O Christ God." Reader: "Glory, Both now", "Lord, have mercy" (3), "Bless." The priest imparts the Small Dismissal, returns into the sanctuary and takes off the epitrachelion. **2. In Concelebration of Two Deacons** **85.** At the end of the Six Psalms, the 1st deacon goes out through the northern doors and exclaims the Great Litany, as well as the verses at "God is the Lord" and, having made a small bow, returns into the sanctuary through the southern doors. After the first Kathisma the 2nd deacon, and after the second Kathisma the 1st deacon, sing the Small Litany. After the Troparia "The Angelic Council" or after the Magnifications, the 2nd deacon likewise sings the Small Litany. After the Gradual [*Stepenna*], the 1st deacon: "Let us be attentive." Priest: "Peace be to all." 2nd deacon: "Wisdom, let us be attentive." 1st deacon: "Let us pray to the Lord." Both deacons cense the holy table, the iconostasis, the choirs and the people, as noted in the rubric of Vespers in concelebration with two deacons (see no. 40). At "Let everything that has breath," the 1st deacon recites the first verse, and the 2nd deacon -- the second. **86.** For the anointing of the faithful, the 1st deacon goes out with the priest, and the 2nd exclaims the Small Litany after the third Ode, and also -- after the sixth Ode. After the Katavasia of the eighth Ode, the 1st deacon takes the censer, goes out through the northern doors, stands before the icon of the Theotokos and calls: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," then returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary and, together with the 2nd deacon, censes the throne and everything else, as noted above (see no. 39), and after the censing they return into the sanctuary: the 1st deacon -- through the southern, and the 2nd -- through the northern doors. After the ninth Ode, the 1st deacon exclaims the Small Litany. **87.** After the Praises stichera, the holy doors are opened. The 1st deacon stands before the holy table to the right of the priest, and the 2nd -- to the left. After the Great Doxology, all, having made a small bow, go to the tetrapod, the priest takes the Holy Gospel and, preceded by the deacons, returns into the sanctuary. **88.** After this, the deacons go out: the 1st deacon exclaims the first litany "Have mercy on us, O God," and the 2nd -- the second "Let us complete." 1st deacon: "Bow your heads unto the Lord." The dismissal follows, which the 1st deacon begins: "Wisdom" (see no. 36, note). **3. Without a Deacon** **89.** A priest who serves Matins without a deacon does and says everything that belongs to the deacon, considering this: **90.** After the completion of the Six Psalms, he does not return into the sanctuary, but, standing before the holy doors, exclaims the Great Litany and its exclamation: "For to Thee belongs," as well as the verses during the singing by the choir of "God is the Lord." After the completion of this, he makes a small bow and returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary. It should be noted that when the priest does not have enough time to recite all the Matins Prayers before the iconostasis, he may finish them in the sanctuary during the reading of the Kathismata. After the Prokimenon, he himself exclaims: "Let us pray to the Lord"; reads the verses at "Let everything that has breath"; before the reading of the Gospel, exclaims: "Let us be attentive," and after the Gospel reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ." After the 3rd and 6th Odes of the Canon -- Small Litanies and exclaims from the throne or from the place where he anoints. After the anointing, he returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary and takes off the phelonion. **91.** After the Katavasia of the 8th Ode, he goes out in the epitrachelion through the northern doors, stands before the icon of the Theotokos and calls: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," then censes, as noted above (see no. 33). After the censing, he returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary, exclaims the Small Litany and its exclamation. **92.** After the Praises stichera, at "Glory," he puts on the phelonion, opens the holy doors and, after the last sticheron, exclaims: "Glory to Thee, who hast shown us the Light." Everything else takes place as was presented (see nos. 82-83). **93.** After the dismissal, the priest closes the holy doors, takes off the phelonion and the 1st Hour begins. **4. In Concelebration of Priests** **94.** Concelebrants, in an even number, put on epitrachelia over their *ryasas* and put on phelons during the reading of the Kathisma and stand on the sides of the holy table, as at Vespers (see no. 50). During the singing of the Polyeleos, all go out, having made a small bow, through the holy doors, having lowered the phelonion and hands down, in front -- the younger ones. Two first ones carry the icon of the feast or saint, if it has not yet been placed on the left side of the tetrapod between both choirs, and place it on the tetrapod; all stand on the sides of the tetrapod in two rows, facing one another, the principal celebrant stands in the middle before the tetrapod, and to his right -- the deacon. **
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9968: Diagram: At the Magnification** [Left Column] Candle-bearer ... 6 ... 4 ... 2 ... Concelebrants [Center] TETRAPOD [Right Column] Candle-bearer ... 5 ... 3 ... 1 ... Concelebrants [Bottom] Celebrant ... Deacon **95.** Then all sing the Magnification. When the choir repeats the Magnification, the priest, preceded by the deacon with a lighted candle, censes from four sides the icon of the feast or saint on the tetrapod, the holy table, the sanctuary, the iconostasis, the concelebrants, both choirs, the people and once more the icon of the feast, but only from the front. If, however, the usual censing takes place before the Gospel, then at the Magnification the priest censes only the icon itself. Choir: "Glory" and again the Magnification. Then the celebrant and concelebrants sing the Magnification once more. **96.** After the Magnifications all, the younger ones -- in front, return through the holy doors into the sanctuary before the holy table. After the Gospel all, having made a small bow, depart and take off their vestments. **Shortening of Matins**^[767] **1. Beginning** -- The priest, having put on the epitrachelion, censes as usual (see at "Lord, I have cried," no. 75). -- Then he exclaims: "Glory to the Holy," and the choir recites everything else and the 142nd Psalm on Sunday or the regular one -- on feasts. -- After the Great Litany -- "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Sunday or Feast, Theotokion according to the tone of the Troparion. **2. Sung Kathismata** -- Regular Kathismata with Sessional Hymns are not taken, but sung ones are taken thus: "The Angelic Council" -- only on Sundays, "Praise the name of the Lord" (Polyeleos) -- only on feasts, "By the waters of Babylon" -- only on the Sundays of Great Lent. -- The priest at the appointed time puts on the phelonion, opens the holy doors and exclaims: "Peace be to all." Then he censes. If there was censing at the Magnification, then before the Reading of the Gospel there will be no censing. -- Prokimenon, "Let everything that has breath" -- according to the tone of the season. **3. Bringing out the Gospel** -- The priest, having read the Gospel, carries the Holy Book out to the tetrapod, accompanied by two candle-bearers. -- The Reader, if there is no deacon, reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ." -- All go to kiss the Gospel. If there was a *Litiya*, then the anointing takes place (see no. 80). -- Having returned to the throne, the priest recites the prayer "O God, save." After this he does not close the holy doors in view of the shortening of Matins. **4. Magnification of the Theotokos** -- Having recited the prayer "O God, save," the priest takes the censer and goes out before the icon of the Theotokos. -- There he exclaims: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," censes the icon, then returns into the sanctuary and censes everything, as usual. -- The choir sings "More honorable than the Cherubim" at the beginning and at the end of the verses, which both choirs recite alternately. **5. Laudatory Prayers** -- Psalms 148, 149 and 150 are sung singly, in turn. -- After "Most Blessed" the priest exclaims: "Glory to Thee, who hast shown us the Light." At the Great Doxology there will be no censing. -- During the singing of the Troparion, the priest carries the Gospel back, and after the completion -- immediately says the dismissal. **Beginning of Paschal Matins** At Paschal Matins the censing takes place with special solemnity. The servers, having put on the best vestments, as at the Divine Liturgy, go out before the throne in the usual order and make a bow all together. The principal celebrant gives the Gospel to the 1st server, and to the 2nd -- the icon of the Resurrection of Christ, he himself takes the cross with his left hand, and with the right (from the deacon) -- the censer. He censes first the throne, and afterwards -- the servers. Then the principal celebrant, having given the censer to the deacon, stands at his place. All bow before the throne and turn to the east to go out to the narthex. The exit takes place according to the usual order of seniority under the peeling of bells and with the singing of the hymn "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior." And the order is such: candle-bearers, priests, deacons with lighted candles, and behind them -- the principal celebrant. All are also given lighted candles. In the narthex all stand before the closed church doors, as in the
Dolnytsky_Typikon_Master.md:9976: [^767]: Such a shortening is generally practiced, therefore we present it here as an appendix. **Chunk 75** **V. Rubric of the Divine Liturgy** **of St. John Chrysostom and St. Basil the Great** **1. In Concelebration of One Deacon** **97.** According to the ancient custom of the Eastern Church, only one Holy Liturgy may be celebrated at the same holy table. However, one may depart from this rule if the Divine Liturgy must be repeated for the benefit of the faithful. If there is one or several secondary churches in the parish, then the more solemn Liturgy is celebrated in the main sanctuary, and other liturgies should be celebrated rather in the side sanctuaries. **98.** The Eucharistic bread does not absolutely have to be baked on the day the Holy Sacrifice is offered. The same bread can be kept for several days and used at several liturgies, provided that all the ceremonies of the Proskomedia are observed over it or over a part of it, cutting the bread and removing the Lamb and particles according to the prescription. Also, it is not necessary to always use five prosphora, if the Lamb and all particles can be taken from one and the same prosphora. The custom of using five prosphora may be kept, if one pleases, when celebrating more solemn liturgies (see no. 179). **99.** The priest who wishes to serve the Sacrament must first of all be reconciled with all and hold nothing against anyone, keep his heart as far as possible from evil thoughts, be continent already from the evening and keep the fast until the very time of the service and prepare himself for the offering of the sacrifice by prayers (see no. 190). **100.** At the prescribed time, the priest enters the church and, together with the deacon, bows three times before the holy doors to the east. Then the deacon says in a low voice: "Bless, Master." Priest: "Blessed is our God." Deacon: "Amen." And the deacon begins to say: "Glory to Thee, our God," "O Heavenly King," "Holy God" (3), "Glory, Both now," "Most Holy Trinity," "Lord, have mercy" (3), "Glory, Both now," "Our Father." Priest: "For Thine is the kingdom." Deacon: "Amen." Then they recite together: "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us," Glory: "Lord, have mercy on us," Both now: "The door of compassion." They approach the icon of the Savior, venerate it with a small bow and, kissing it, say: "We worship Thine immaculate Icon." Having bowed before the holy doors, in the same manner they venerate and kiss the icon of the Theotokos, saying the troparion "Forasmuch as thou art a well-spring of compassion." **101.** After this the priest and deacon return before the holy doors, and the priest, bowing his head, recites the prayer "O Lord, stretch forth Thy hand." Then the priest and deacon bow also to the right and left choirs and go into the sanctuary: the priest -- through the southern, and the deacon -- through the northern doors, saying: "I will enter into Thy house" (see no. 178). **102.** Having entered the sanctuary, they make three small bows before the holy table and kiss: the priest -- the Holy Gospel, and the deacon -- the holy table and, if there exists a custom, both -- the altar cross.
