Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:476: Question I: How do we know how many Sundays there will be in a given year between Theophany and the Triodion?
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:506: According to this, if you wish to know which Gospel you must take this year 1899 after Theophany before the Triodion, then, having looked at the table of boundary keys, which is at the end of the Horologion or of this typikon, you will find for this year the boundary key "Щ"; also having looked at the table presented here before, you will find this letter in the fifth row, and on the right side of the table - the number "IV". Therefore, between Theophany and the Triodion there will be four Sundays, of which the first will be the Sunday after the Enlightenment, the second - the sequential 30th, the third - the 31st Sunday, the fourth - the 32nd Sunday, which is about Zacchaeus; also the Triodion.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:507: Question 2: How many sequential Sundays can be superfluous, and how many can be lacking before the Triodion?
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:790: After the singing of the three stichera there will be three readings: the first - of the Prophecy with a Prokimenon, the second - of the Apostle without a Prokimenon and without Alleluia, the third - of the Gospel, beginning it from "Wisdom, attend!", "Let us hear". All this is accompanied by the following actions: when the stichera end, the Priest puts on a phelonion and, having gone out before the steps of the altar and having bowed, opens the Holy Doors and exclaims: "Let us attend! Peace be unto all!" (blessing the people), "Wisdom! Let us attend!". The choirs sing the Prokimenon. After its conclusion the Priest, having exclaimed before the title of the Prophecy "Wisdom!" and before the reading "Let us attend!", waits before the steps of the altar for the end of the reading of the Prophecy and, after its conclusion, having exclaimed "Wisdom!" and "Let us attend!" before the reading of the Apostle, as usual, puts incense into the censer with the short prayer "Incense we offer to Thee" and, having blessed the incense with the words "Blessed is our God", censes everything as usual, that is the Holy Table from four sides, the iconostasis, the choirs and the people[^316]. At the end of the Apostle, and together with it the censing, the candle-bearers go out before the analogion, and the Priest approaches the Holy Table and, having bowed, takes the Holy Gospel and, having placed it on the analogion, begins to sing the Gospel from "Wisdom, attend". After the singing of the Gospel the Priest, having kissed the beginning of the reading, leaves it there, closes the Holy Doors and, having bowed low to the Holy Mysteries, goes out to the sacristy, takes off the phelonion, goes out through the side doors before the Holy Doors for "Thou Who at all times" and remains there until the end of the Hour.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:792: If there be deacons, they put on dalmatics with an orarion before the beginning of the 1st Hour and remain vested until the end of the Typika. At the Prokimenon, together with the Priest, they go before the steps of the altar and, having bowed low all together to the Holy Mysteries, open the Holy Doors. The right Deacon exclaims: "Let us attend!", and the left, after the giving of peace by the Priest: "Wisdom! Let us attend!". Also before the first and second reading - the right: "Wisdom!", and the left: "Let us attend!" and they cense everything as usual, as at the beginning of the Liturgy. At the end of the Apostle, and together with it the censing, both deacons stand with the Priest before the steps of the altar and, having bowed all three together to the Holy Mysteries, go out from the sanctuary: the Priest with the Gospel through the middle [doors], and the deacons, having swapped their sides, through the side doors to the analogion. The right Deacon exclaims: "Wisdom, attend!", "Let us hear", the Priest: "The reading from the Holy Gospel according to (Name)", and the left deacon: "Let us attend!". The Priest sings the Gospel. Having finished, the Priest kisses the beginning of the reading, also the right deacon, and after him the left. Having bowed low, the deacons return through their doors to the sanctuary and, together with the Priest, close the Holy Doors. Having bowed low all three together to the Holy Mysteries, they withdraw: the deacons to their places, and the Priest before the iconostasis for "Thou Who at all times".
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:794: Question: When the Royal Hours are transferred from Sunday or Saturday back, to Friday, should one take on them the Troparion of the Eve or of the Forefeast, 22 or 23 December?
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:795: Answer: The Typikon mentions the Troparion and Kontakion of the Eve, therefore we think that one should take [those] of the Eve. If someone thinks that this will not be good and takes one Troparion for two days, that is on the Eve and before the Eve, let him consider that the Troparion of the Forefeast is also taken the same on two different days of the Forefeast.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:861: ##### 8.	There will be 8 readings, and on Theophany - 11. After the first three and the second three readings of both feasts there will be a separate troparion with its verses; there will be three of them[^321], besides "Glory, Both now", for the whole troparion is sung only twice, namely: once before the first verse and once before the last verse, that is before "Both now", and after the other verses - only up to the half[^322].
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:881: ##### 2.	During the introductory Psalm the Priest, standing before the Holy Doors, says the evening prayers, as usual, and the deacons stand beside him and, at the end of the Psalm, one of them (the right) sings the Great Litany, and the Priest - its exclamation, and then, all bow low and withdraw to the sanctuary. If there be no deacons, then the Priest himself sings the litany.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:1143: Now, if you wish to know how many Saturdays and Sundays there will be in a given year between the Nativity and Theophany, then first of all you must find out on which day of the week the Nativity of Christ falls, afterwards, going along the transverse line from that day of the week to Theophany, you will find the number of Saturdays and Sundays between the Nativity and Theophany. Regarding the rule which will be for these Saturdays and Sundays in the case when there will be two Saturdays and two Sundays (as in the 4th, 5th and 6th rows), there will be no inconsistency, as there will be no inconsistency in the case when either two Saturdays, or two Sundays (as in the 3rd and 7th rows), for these two Saturdays and two Sundays have their unchangeable rule. But all inconsistencies are limited to the case of only one Saturday or Sunday (as in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd and 7th rows). In this case two questions arise:
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:1148: To the first question we answer: if this Saturday or Sunday is in the middle of the feast of the Nativity, that is within December, then the Saturday or Sunday after the Nativity is taken; if - outside the feast of the Nativity, that is within January, then the Saturday or Sunday before Theophany is taken. Regarding the second question, that is what to do with one Saturday or Sunday which sometimes has no place, we answer: Greek books give no rule for this and therefore this Saturday or Sunday should not have been taken. However, the Slavs do not wish to discard anything and give the following rules for this case.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:1334: After the Prayer behind the Ambo, if Saturday or Sunday, then after the dismissal of Vespers, the church cross-bearer stands between two candle-bearers in the middle of the church, and before Him - a church server with three candlesticks, with a censer and with a vessel for incense. The Deacon takes the Holy Gospel from the table, the Priest takes the cross with his right hand and, having bowed low to the Holy Mysteries, they go out through the Holy Doors: first the Deacon with the Gospel, behind him - the Priest with the cross, vested in all sacred vestments of his rank, and the Choir sings the Troparion "The voice of the Lord"[^347]; all proceed to the narthex of the church. When all have gone out there, the Deacon places the Holy Gospel, and the Priest - the cross on a table covered with a tablecloth or a clean cloth, on which there will be also a wide vessel with water; and immediately the Priest censes the table from four sides and all present, according to the order.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:1335: After the end of the troparia follow three readings of prophecies; also the Apostle with its Prokimenon and Alleluia, during the singing of which the deacons cense the water from four sides of the tetrapod, the Priest and all present, according to the order. Then the Priest reads the Gospel, before which the first Deacon exclaims: "Wisdom, attend" and the rest, and the second: "Let us attend".
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:1338: At the end of this prayer the Choir: "Amen"; priest: "Peace be unto all" and, after the prayer at the bowing of heads, which he says quietly, he exclaims: "For Thou Art the sanctification" and immediately blesses with the cross: immerses it simply into the water, holding with both hands, and sings this troparion: "When in Jordan"[^352], and all the people sing with Him. "And the second time singing the same troparion"[^353], and all the people sing with Him. Then the server pours holy water onto a tray or into a sprinkler. The Priest turns his face to the west, holding the precious cross with his left hand, and with the right - the sprinkler, and sprinkles all the people with holy water. After this the choirs sing Glory, Both now: Stichera "Let us praise, O faithful", the procession returns to the church in the same way, and the Priest sprinkles on all sides[^354], and thus they enter the sanctuary through the Holy Doors: the Deacon with the Gospel, and after him the Priest with the cross. And when the Priest has sprinkled the entire sanctuary, the Deacon places the Gospel, and the Priest the cross on the Holy Table, in its place, and the Choir sings "Blessed be the name of the Lord", if there be Vespers with the Service of Basil; if Vespers - separate from the Holy Liturgy, then immediately the Great Dismissal of the Feast; at the end: Troparion, Glory, Both now: Kontakion of the Feast, which both choirs sing together in the middle of the church, as at Vespers with the Service of Basil on 25 December.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:1855: Everything according to the rule given before for the Forefeast here on p. 214, only at Matins, besides the stichera of the Feast at the Aposticha, there will be also stichera of the Triodion at the Praises with Small Doxology.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:3801: The Procession of the Precious Cross, which took place once in Constantinople from the imperial palace to the most beautiful church of Sophia, we depict now by the bringing out of the Precious Cross in the evening first from the sacristy to the Holy Table, and then, on the day of 1 August, from the Holy Table to the tetrapod, at Matins during the Doxology, when the Veneration also takes place, according to the rule of 14 September, on p. 120, except the Exaltation, which happens only on 14 September on the Exaltation of the Precious Cross, and here there will be none. Besides this, in Slavic books on this day also the Lesser Blessing of Water is prescribed either at Matins immediately before the Veneration, or later, at another time of the day. In some places also the blessing of herbs takes place, as on the Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos[^454].
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:3814: ##### 4.	During the singing of the Praises the Priest, having vested in all sacred vestments (of violet color)[^455]  and having exclaimed before the steps of the Holy Table: "Glory to Thee, Who Hast shown us the light", and having censed the precious Cross, carries it out around the Holy Table through the northern doors before the Holy Doors, and after the end of the Great Doxology with the Trisagion, exclaims: "Wisdom, attend", goes before the tetrapod and, having placed the precious Cross on it, when the Choir sings the Troparion "Save, O Lord", censes it from four sides and venerates the precious Cross, everything exactly as on 14 September, here on p. 122-127.
Final_Dolnytsky_part3_menaion.md:3823: ##### 1.	On Saturday evening, after the Dismissal of Vespers the Bringing Out of the Precious Cross from the sacristy to the Holy Table takes place, and at Matins, during the Great Doxology, from the Holy Table to the tetrapod, according to the rule of 14 September. However, after the exclamation "Wisdom, attend", instead of the Troparion "Save, O Lord", we sing the Resurrectional Troparion, one of the two usual ones, at which the Priest should cense the Precious Cross from four sides and immediately after this perform the veneration of the Precious Cross, singing the Troparion "Before Thy Cross we bow down", or before the veneration perform also the rite of the Blessing of Water, according to the rule given here for weekdays, no. 5.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:229: Regarding the refrains to the troparia of the canon, know that in order to find the number of refrains as quickly as possible, the last four, six, and eight verses of the Biblical Odes (except the 2nd, for it is read in full before the heirmos) from the bottom up are marked in the middle of the song, or on the side, with the inscription: "on 4, on 6, on 8". But on 14 they are not counted off, since 6 verses from 8 upwards are easily counted. However, the counting of 14 verses in current Psalters does not coincide exactly with the counting in previous Psalters, for where the rubrics place the 14th verse at "The waters became thick", current Psalters place the 14th verse at "The enemy said", and "The waters became thick" moves to the 15th verse. The 6th Ode has only 12 verses, so do not forget to divide these two verses of it on 4. The 9th Ode, one, of the Theotokos, is sung whole before the heirmos with "More honorable", and the other, of Zachariah, is sung whole in the refrains, and in those editions where there are only 13 verses, one verse must be divided into 2.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:305: 7.	"Let my prayer be set forth". Has after it three verses and is sung six times, that is: twice - before the first verse, once - after each verse and again once at the end. And among us the first time "Let my prayer be set forth" is sung by the Priest, the second time - by the Choir, all three verses - by the Priest, after each the Choir - "Let my prayer be set forth", the last time in a higher voice up to the half - the Priest, and from the half - the Choir. Where there are two choirs, they sing "Let my prayer be set forth" alternately: the first begins after the Priest, and the second half is sung by both choirs together. When the Priest sings, he himself stands, and the Choir and people kneel; when the Choir sings, the Choir stands together with the people, and the Priest kneels. If there are two choirs, the one singing stands, and the other kneels; people who are on the side of the right choir kneel, stand and sing with the right choir, and those on the left - with the left[^531].
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:306: The Priest sings "Let my prayer be set forth" first in front of the altar, then the 1st verse - on the south, the 2nd - on the west, the 3rd - on the north side, and at the end the first half - again in front of the holy table[^532]. He censes each side of the altar twice, that is before the beginning of his singing and at the end of the Choir's singing, that is at "The lifting up of my hands", for then, having censed, he moves to the next side of the altar, in order to cense there also, so that the Choir is ready to sing its verse[^533].
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:517: ##### 10.	On the Sunday of the Resurrection very early it is fitting to sing Matins with all the rites that precede and accompany it, according to the rubric approved by this Synod. In cathedral churches, similarly as in non-cathedral ones, this Matins is usually anticipated and celebrated on Saturday evening; the Synod considers that such a custom may be adhered to.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:549: ##### 2.	The blessing and distribution of branches takes place, according to the rubric, immediately after the 50th Psalm, during the kissing of the Gospel. According to our custom, the blessing takes place after the 50th Psalm, and the distribution - during the anointing with oil (myrovanie), that is during the canon. And it takes place thus: When the Priest has sung the Gospel, he places it on the analogion near the tetrapod, between two candles lit on candlesticks. On the tetrapod are placed branches and prepared censers and sprinklers. The Priest approaches the tetrapod and at the time when the Choir reads the 50th Psalm, having put incense in and blessed the censer, censes the branches from the four sides of the tetrapod and, after the conclusion of the 50th Psalm, when the Deacon exclaims (if there be none, then the Priest himself) "Let us pray to the Lord", reads aloud (from the Sluzhebnik (lit. "Service Book") or from the Triodion[^563] ) the first prayer "O Lord our God", and the Choir responds: "Amen". Priest: "Peace be to all"; deacon: "Bow your heads unto the Lord"; and, after "To Thee, O Lord", the Priest reads the second prayer "O Lord God Almighty... unto ages of ages"[^564]; choir: "Amen"; the Priest sprinkles the branches with holy water, saying: "These branches are blessed and sanctified" and the rest; and, after singing the litany "Save, O God", with His right hand anoints the people, and with His left hand distributes to all the branches, which the Deacon solemnly hands to him, and if there be no deacon, then one of the church servers[^565].
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:616: "It is fitting for the Priest on Great Thursday at the Liturgy to consecrate two Lambs, in order to consume one at Communion, and to place the other in the artophorion and keep in the tabernacle in the usual place until today (Friday)". Thus in our Sluzhebnik[^584]. Regarding the Lamb itself, which is to be kept in the tabernacle until the day of exposition, the Synod of Lviv orders that "it before consecration be fitted to its vessel, the so-called Melchizedek, into which it is well inserted and carefully wiped, so that afterwards crumbs do not fall from it; so that placed in such a way in the Melchizedek, it be consecrated at the Liturgy of one of the preceding days and kept in the tabernacle until the exposition itself". When the Priest prepares the Lamb at the Proskomedia, wishing to use it for exposition, he does not say over it the words "In remembrance of the Lord" etc., nor does He perform over it the sacred actions which are usually performed over the Lamb that is to be consumed at the same Divine Service, for it is considered only communicative; it is enough only to cut it, so that it would be convenient to break and consume it on another day.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:634: From the usual beginning to the end of the troparion after "Alleluia" and from the Canon to the end of Matins everything - according to the rubric of Matins of Great Thursday, here on pp. 383-384 [→REF:p383-384]. Between the troparion and the canon there will be five Sessional Hymns, each of which has before it three antiphons of the Passion troparia, without Kathismata and small litanies, then the Beatitudes, Prokimenon and Psalm 50. Besides this we read 12 Passion Gospels: the first - after the troparion, the five following - after each of the five Sessional Hymns, the seventh - after the Beatitudes, the eighth - after the 50th Psalm, the ninth - after the Exaposteilarion, the tenth - after the Praises, the eleventh - after "Let us complete", the twelfth - after the Aposticha. After the singing of each Gospel we ring all the church bells and, after the twelfth Gospel, we do not ring until the Resurrection itself, but instead of bells we use wooden clappers. But it is better already at Passion Matins not to ring, but to strike clappers or boards, for Passion Matins is a time of sorrow, to which the joyful sounds of bells do not correspond. Also in Rome already at Passion Matins clappers are used.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:640: All this - exactly so at the readings of the other Gospels, with the exception of the litany and censing, which according to the rubric of our Triodia and the Typikon of the Lviv University, will be only at the 1st Gospel and at the five Sessional Hymns, and the censing - only of the holy table. They give thus: "We do not sit at these Sessional Hymns, since the Priest censes the holy table, but we sing them standing"[^588]. Finally we consider that it is fitting to cense not only at the Sessional Hymns, but also at all other Gospels, for the censing takes place not for the sake of the Sessional Hymns, but for the sake of the Gospels, however, only the holy table (around), as our Triodia have, which mention the censing only of the holy table. Everything should be censed only twice, that is at the 1st Gospel and at the Canon, at the heirmos of the 9th Ode.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:642: If there be deacons, they remain from the beginning of Matins, dressed in dalmatic with orarion of violet color, in the sanctuary, at their places, until they begin to sing the third time the troparion "When the glorious disciples"; then they come out together with the Priest who will sing the Gospel, dressed in a violet phelonion, before the steps of the altar and, having bowed together with him to the Holy Mysteries, open the holy doors, cense everything as usual, as at the beginning of the Liturgy, return before the steps of the altar and, having given up the censers and bowed both together to the Holy Mysteries, go out, each through his door, before the holy doors and, again having bowed to the Holy Mysteries, stand there until the second deacon after the singing of the troparion sings the Small Litany; then, having bowed to the Holy Mysteries and having exchanged their places, they proceed to the analogion so that, when the Priest approaches the analogion, the first Deacon is on the right, and the second - on the left of him. When the Priest, having bowed to the Holy Mysteries, comes out through the holy doors to the analogion, the first Deacon exclaims: "And that we may be accounted worthy". Priest: "Peace be to all". Second deacon: "Wisdom, Aright. Let us listen". Priest: "The Reading from (Name)". First deacon: "Let us attend". Priest - reading. After the conclusion of the reading, the Priest kisses the beginning of the reading first himself, afterwards the Deacon who is on the right kisses [it], and after him the one who is on the left. All three return to the sanctuary: the Priest - through the central, and the deacons - through the side doors, stand together with the Priest before the steps of the altar and, having closed the holy doors and bowed all together to the Holy Mysteries, go to their places. At the other Gospels, since there will be no Small Litany, the deacons, having come out before the holy doors, bow and, having exchanged places, proceed to the analogion and, when the Priest stands between them, exclaim: "And that we may be accounted worthy" and the rest, as above.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:678: ##### 5.	At the last sticheron of the Aposticha, that is at "Thee, Who Art clothed with light", takes place the procession with the Shroud and the placing of it in the Tomb. According to the custom of our cathedral churches this takes place in the following way: During the singing of the Aposticha the procession is formed in the church. On the altar, on the left side, stands the monstrance, candles are lit on the altar and at the Tomb.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:679: The sacred ministers, the Bishop and the four most worthy canons, who will carry the Shroud, vest in all sacred vestments of their rank, and the other canons and priests - only in epitrachelia and phelonia, some - only in epitrachelia, and two deacons - in all diaconal [vestments]. Lit candles are distributed to the canons and other priests in the sanctuary, and to the brotherhood - in the church. The sacred ministers approach the steps of the altar and bow low. The Bishop ascends to the altar, takes the Shroud, spread on the altar, and gives it to the four canons so that each holds it by one corner. Then the Deacon who is on the right ascends to the altar and, having bowed low, unlocks the artophorion, takes from there the pyx with the Holy Mysteries, places [it] on the (folded) antimension, takes from there the Lamb with the stand into which it is inserted, not touching it with his fingers, but holding by the stand, places [it] into the monstrance and locks it well. Having locked the pyx, he places [it] into the artophorion and, having bowed, locks it also; and places the monstrance on the antimension so that it faces the people, steps aside until the Bishop, kneeling, censes the Holy Mysteries three times, each time - with a prostration at the end, and, having taken upon himself the omophorion (aer), approaches the altar. Then the Deacon, having bowed to the Holy Mysteries, takes with both hands the monstrance and hands it to the Bishop, who before that makes a prostration. The Bishop turns to the right to the people, and when the singers begin to sing "Thee, Who Art clothed with light", goes out behind the clergy through the holy doors on the northern side of the Tomb into the church and, having entered under the canopy, surrounded by candle-bearers and preceded by two deacons who cense unceasingly, proceeds behind all to the church doors. In front goes the cross-bearer, then the banner-bearers, four girls icon-bearers, after them - the church brotherhood, the seminarians, the seminary choir, then, from the holy doors, - priests, canons, four sacred dignitaries who carry the Shroud, turned with feet to the people, and at the end - the Bishop with deacons who cense, and candle-bearers. The procession goes out of the church and turns to the right, that is to its northern side and, having gone around the church three times (or, as now they do there, - once), returns to the church. Those who carry the Shroud, and the other priests stand before the Tomb, and the Bishop with the deacons returns by the southern side of the Tomb, through the holy doors, to the sanctuary. The right deacon receives from his hands the Holy Mysteries and places [them] on the altar. The Bishop kneels and, having put aside the omophorion (Velum), censes the Holy Mysteries three times, each time bowing his head; the Deacon, having bowed, exposes the Holy Mysteries at the top of the tabernacle. Having bowed before the steps of the altar, all three depart: the Bishop through the central, the deacons - through the side doors before the Tomb. The Bishop receives the Shroud from the canons who hold it, and places [it] on the Tomb, and they fasten it to the Tomb. When the singers have finished singing "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart", the Trisagion and the rest with "Our Father", the Bishop exclaims: "For Thine is the Kingdom" and begins to sing with the servers the troparion "The noble Joseph"; at the end he makes with the servers a low bow, and all the people - a great prostration to the ground. Then the second time the same troparion - the second choir, and all - a prostration, as before. And where there will be only one choir, the first time the troparion is sung by the sacred ministers, the second time - by the Choir, the third time the half - by the sacred ministers, and from "wrapped [it]" and further - by the Choir[^601]. After the singing of the troparion and after the prostrations all kiss the Shroud, according to the order, having made before the kissing one prostration: first the Bishop, and after him - all other sacred ministers, according to the rank of their dignity. After this there will be the Great Dismissal, specific to this Saturday, after which the sacred ministers return to the sanctuary, leaving the holy doors open until the end of Bright Week, take off the sacred vestments and go out, and the people kiss the Shroud[^602].
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:705: The Entrance takes place thus: During the singing of the Stichera of the Praises the Priest and deacons, if there be any, having bowed low before the Shroud, and simultaneously also before the Holy Mysteries, go to the sacristy and vest in all sacred vestments of their rank. During the singing of the Doxastikon also the other sacred ministers, who are before the Tomb of God, dressed in phelonia, having bowed low, depart to the sanctuary and, when the Priest with the deacons comes before the steps of the altar, stand together with him on both sides and bow all together to the Holy Mysteries. After singing the Praises [Stichera] the Priest exclaims: "Glory to Thee Who Hast shown us the light", then puts incense in for the left deacon and, when the Doxology ends, approaches the altar, takes from it the Gospel, gives it to the right deacon and, preceded by candle-bearers and the Deacon with the censer, they make the Entrance just as at the Liturgy. Having stood before the Tomb of God: the right deacon - on the right, and the left - on the left of the Priest, closer to the Tomb, the other sacred ministers - on two wings, a little at a distance, await the conclusion of the Trisagion and the sticheron "Come, let us bless". After the conclusion of the singing of the sticheron the first deacon, raising the orarion in the direction of the people, exclaims: "Let us attend", the Priest, blessing the people: "Peace be to all", the second deacon, raising the orarion: "Wisdom, let us attend" and we sing the Troparion "The noble Joseph" once. The first deacon places the Gospel on the analogion facing the people, the second gives up the censer and the Troparion of the Prophecy once[^607].
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:718: ##### 5.	Throughout the whole Bright Week, beginning from the Vespers of this day, the Resurrection service is sung and, besides the stichera of the Triodion, are sung also the Resurrection stichera of the Octoechos, each day - in a different tone, that is on Sunday - in Tone 1, on Monday - in Tone 2, on Tuesday - in Tone 3, on Wednesday - in Tone 4, on Thursday - in Tone 5, on Friday - in Tone 6, on Saturday - in Tone 8, omitting Tone 7.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:735: After the Shroud is taken from the Tomb of God and transferred to the sanctuary, the sacred ministers, having dressed in all the brightest sacred vestments of their rank, go out before the steps of the altar and, having bowed all together low to the Holy Mysteries, bend their knees and receive lit candles; so also the brotherhood in the church. The Deacon, who is on the right (or the Priest himself, if there be no deacons) approaches the middle of the altar and, having bowed low, places the Gospel on its right side, then, having taken the Holy Mysteries from the tabernacle and placed them on the antimension, steps aside a little, and the Bishop (or main priest), having censed the Holy Mysteries three times, as usual, and having taken upon himself the omophorion, rises, ascends the steps and, having bowed, receives the Holy Mysteries, after which turns to the people and begins to sing the sticheron "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior". Under the joyful sound of all bells the procession around the church takes place, with everything, as was given on Great Friday at Vespers, here on page 394, except the Shroud, which now, by the decision of bl. m. Cardinal Sembratovych, should not be carried, for this does not correspond to the joy of the Resurrection; rather it is fitting to carry the icon of the Resurrection, as it is in the Triodion, at the beginning of Resurrection Matins, during the procession to the church doors. Therefore, when the main sacred minister begins to sing the sticheron "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior", the choirs and people take up the singing and sing it, repeating, all the time, until the procession ends, namely the circuit of the church either three times, or at least once. When the circuit ends, all enter the church; in order, however, to avoid confusion during the second procession, those carrying banners and icons remain before the church doors, and in the church prepare for the procession the cross-bearer with two candle-bearers, the brotherhood, choirs and clergy. When the sacred ministers enter the sanctuary, the main priest ascends to the altar and, having turned to the people, blesses them with the Holy Mysteries, quietly saying: "Save, O God, Thy people and bless Thine inheritance", places the Holy Mysteries on the altar, descends before the steps, bends the knees and, having taken off the omophorion, censes the Holy Mysteries three times, as usual; the Deacon, having approached the Holy Mysteries, bows low, takes them with the stand from the monstrance and places into the artophorion, and places the artophorion into the tabernacle and, having bowed, locks it. And where there will be no monstrance, the Holy Mysteries are placed in the artophorion and with it are locked in the tabernacle.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:749: If there be only one priest, he stands in the holy doors and from there gives the cross to be kissed by the people, greeting everyone: "Christ is risen", and the one Who kisses answers: "Truly He is risen". If there be several priests, then first the main one goes out from the holy doors and stands before the iconostasis, on the right side, that is opposite the icon of the Savior, looking to the north. After Him the second priest comes out from the holy doors with the Gospel, approaches the main one, and he gives him the cross to kiss, saying: "Christ is risen", having kissed, in His turn, gives Him the Gospel to kiss, answering: "Truly He is risen" and stands on His left. Then comes out the third priest with the icon of the Resurrection of Christ, approaches the first and He gives Him the cross to kiss, saying: "Christ is risen", and having kissed gives Him the icon of the Resurrection to kiss, answering: "Truly He is risen", and approaches the second priest, who gives him the Gospel to kiss, saying: "Christ is risen", and He, having kissed the Gospel, gives the second to kiss the icon of the Resurrection, answering: "Truly He is risen", and stands on the left of the second. The fourth priest comes out with the icon of the Theotokos, approaches the first, also the second and the third, of whom each gives him his object to kiss, saying: "Christ is risen", and having kissed gives each of them the icon of the Theotokos to kiss, answering: "Truly He is risen". So do also all other priests, as many as there be. In cathedral churches the Bishop, sitting on his throne opposite the icon of the Savior, gives the cross to be kissed, also each of the canons gives His insignia to be kissed, sitting on the seat. And when the kissing between the priests ends, then other sacred ministers and all the people approach one by one each Priest in turn, starting from the first, and kiss his insignia, answering: "Truly He is risen"[^622]. After the kissing once was read the "Homily" of Chrysostom, which is in the Flower Triodion.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:761: "Let it be known also that throughout the whole Bright Week the great holy doors of the iconostasis and the small side ones in all churches remain open also during communion" (Rubric of our Pochaiv Triodion and Russian Typika).
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:858: Since no rubric gives anything about this, we consider that it should be thus: At the 1st - Troparion "The noble Joseph", at the 3rd - Sunday and Glory: of the saint, at the 6th - to the Myrrh-bearers, at the 9th - again "The noble Joseph" and Glory: of the saint. Kontakia: at the 1st and 6th - of the Myrrh-bearers, at the 3rd and 9th - of the saint. If there be two saints, then at the 3rd, after "Glory", will be the Troparion to the first, and at the 9th - to the second saint[^648].
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:1081: The Priest, on one of the days preceding the feast, let him consecrate at the Liturgy two Lambs: one let him consume at Communion, and the other let him place in the tabernacle and keep until the day of the feast. All this let him do according to the rubric given for Great Thursday here, on page 385, in the note. On the day of the feast itself, after the Proskomedia, immediately before the beginning of the Divine Liturgy, after the censing of everything as usual by deacons (or by the Priest himself), the exposition of the Holy Mysteries takes place in the following way: the first deacon (who is on the right of the Priest) places the Gospel on the right side of the holy table, unlocks the artophorion and, having bowed low to the Holy Mysteries, takes from the artophorion the pyx, all bend their knees, and he places it on the folded antimension; if there be a monstrance, then he unlocks the pyx, takes from it the Lamb, not touching it with his fingers, but holding by the stand on which it lies, places it into the groove of the monstrance, locks it well, then places the pyx into the artophorion, bows before it and, having locked the artophorion, places the monstrance, turned with face side to the people, on the antimension and steps aside a little to the right side. The Priest puts incense into the censer, blesses it, saying: "Blessed is our God", and censes with both hands the Holy Mysteries three times, bowing His head at each censing. Then, having taken upon Himself with the help of the second deacon the omophorion, approaches the holy table and, having bowed low, with the help of the first Deacon takes with both hands with the omophorion the monstrance and, having turned it with the reverse side to himself, turns his right side to the people and sings solemnly and majestically the beginning of the first strophe: "O saving Victim", and all people continue to sing the same strophe. And again the Priest - the beginning of the second strophe: "To the One God in Trinity", and all sing the rest. The Priest signs the people crosswise with the Holy Mysteries, then goes in a semicircle from the southern side to the northern and, having returned by the northern to the altar, places the monstrance on the antimension, having turned it with the help of the Deacon with face side to the people. Then, having stepped aside a little to the left, descends before the steps of the altar so as not to turn his back to the Holy Mysteries, kneels and, having put aside the omophorion and having censed again three times the Holy Mysteries, as at the beginning, waits, kneeling, until the Deacon, having approached the middle of the altar and bowed low, places the Holy Mysteries on the tabernacle. Then the Priest, having ascended the steps, begins the Liturgy from "Blessed is the Kingdom", "O Heavenly King" and the rest. During the Liturgy the sacred ministers never turn their backs to the Holy Mysteries, as our Sluzhebnyky prescribe: "When (the Priest), according to the ritual prescriptions, has to turn to the people, he should not turn from the middle of the altar, so as not to turn his back to the Body of Christ, but, having bowed low and stepped aside a little to the left side, from this side turns to the people and, having turned again to the altar, bows low to the Body of Christ, and then, when the Priest has to make a full turn, as at 'Always, now and ever', when he turns with the Holy Mysteries, then he does not make a full turn, so as not to turn his back to the Body of Christ"[^662]. Let us add also that when the Priest or deacon goes out before the iconostasis for censing, he does not cense from the middle, nor turns his back to the Holy Mysteries, but stands a little on the side, the Priest - from the side of the Theotokos, and the deacons: one - from the side of the Savior, and the other - of the Theotokos.
Final_Dolnytsky_part4_triodion.md:1088: After the conclusion of the Prayer behind the Ambo the sacred ministers stand before the steps of the altar, and the first deacon ascends the steps and, having bowed low, takes down the Holy Mysteries from the tabernacle, places on the folded antimension and steps aside a little to his side. All kneel, and the Priest, having censed three times the Holy Mysteries and having taken upon himself the omophorion, ascends the steps, with the help of the Deacon takes with both hands the Holy Mysteries, turns to the people, as at the beginning of the Liturgy, and, having begun the troparion "Bread transubstantiated" or some other pious hymn, preceded by two deacons with censers, enters under the canopy and proceeds to the church doors to the sounds of bells and the procession from the church to the first altar begins, and the Choir and people continue to sing the troparion or something else. The order of the procession is such. In front goes the cross-bearer between two candle-bearers, then banner- and icon-bearers, then four girls with icons, after them the church brotherhood, two by two, with candles, choirs and all the clergy, two by two, with candles, according to their dignity: the nearest priests - in phelonia, and others - in epitrachelia, the last - the main priest with the Holy Mysteries, preceded by two deacons, who from the sides unceasingly cense, and candle-bearers surrounding the canopy. When the procession arrives at the first altar and stops near it, all the clergy divides into two wings, the canopy is placed opposite the altar, and the Priest with the Holy Mysteries ascends to the altar and, with the help of the Deacon, places the Holy Mysteries on the folded antimension; then, having bent his knees, censes them three times and, having risen, steps aside a little to the left and turns to the people. Then the Gospel-bearers approach the Priest, the Priest opens the Gospel at the place of the 1st reading, and the first Deacon exclaims: "And that we may be accounted worthy" etc., choir responds "Lord, have mercy" (3), priest sings: "Peace be to all"; second deacon: "Wisdom, Aright. Let us listen" etc., priest: "The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to (Name)" and, when the first Deacon exclaims: "Let us attend", the Priest reads the first Gospel (Matthew, Section 108. 17-30). After the conclusion of the reading he kisses its beginning and, having bent his knees before the Holy Mysteries, sings (if there is a custom) with other sacred ministers the first member of the supplicatory song "Holy God" three times and all people - the same three times. Then, having again censed the Holy Mysteries three times and risen, bows low, takes the Holy Mysteries with both hands and, having turned to the right to the people blesses them crosswise once (bishop - three times), saying quietly: "Save, O God, Thy people and bless Thine inheritance", and so, to the singing of the festal troparion or other hymn, the Priest enters under the canopy and the procession moves to the second altar, from it - to the third, then - to the fourth, and at each everything takes place exactly as at the first, only the members of the supplicatory song and the readings of the Gospels are changed, of which the second will be from Mark, Section 64.12-26; the third - from Luke, Section 108.7-22; the fourth - from John, Section 23.48-58. If there be several priests, they read the Gospels alternately. After this the procession returns to the church and the Priest, having placed the Holy Mysteries on the altar, kneels, censes them three times, rises and, having taken the Holy Mysteries and turned to the people, sings solemnly and majestically the verse of the Ambrosian hymn: "Save Thy people, O Lord" (3), and the people repeat the first and second time the same, and when the Priest sings it the third time and finishes with the words "and bless Thine inheritance" blessing the people once, then the people sing the further verses of this hymn, and the Priest, having turned with the northern side to the altar and placed the Holy Mysteries on it, descends before the steps, puts aside the omophorion, kneels and censes them three times. Then the right deacon approaches the Holy Mysteries, bows low, takes from the tabernacle the pyx and, having taken the Lamb from the monstrance, places it with the stand into the pyx, and places it into the tabernacle and, having again bowed, locks the tabernacle and places the Gospel on the antimension. If there be no deacon, then the Priest himself does all this. Then all rise, and the Choir sings "Blessed be the name of the Lord" and the rest of the Liturgy, as usual.
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:10: Before the beginning of Vespers, the Priest, dressed in all sacerdotal [vestments], and the Deacon - in all diaconal vestments, having come before the steps of the holy table, open the holy doors and the first deacon, having laid aside the Gospel to the right side, takes the Holy Mysteries from the tabernacle [kyvot] and places Them on the iliton; the Priest censes Them thrice over all, as at the beginning of the Liturgy, places Them on the skynia^[A] on the folded iliton and, having placed the Gospel again in the middle of the holy table, exclaims: "Bless, Master," and the Priest, standing with the deacons at the holy table, begins: "Blessed is our God." Vespers is sung with an Entrance. The Priest departs from the holy table only at the Entrance, and the deacons - also at the litanies and after the prokimenon for the sermon, if there be one; then the Holy Mysteries are covered with a veil.
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:11: Before the dismissal of Vespers, all kneel, and the Deacon takes the Holy Gospel from the middle of the holy table and, having placed it on the right side, removes the Holy Mysteries from the skynia and places [Them] on the folded iliton. The Priest censes Them thrice with a bowing of the head and, having given away the censer, takes upon himself the omophorets [humeral veil], approaches the Holy Mysteries and, having bowed low, takes Them with both hands, with the help of the Deacon, turns right to the people, begins some pious hymn and goes out through the holy doors into the church; he enters under the baldachin and proceeds to the church doors, preceded by deacons with censers, who incessantly cense the Holy Mysteries from the sides; after them follow other priests in phelons, some - only in epitrachelia, with lighted candles in their hands, in two wings, likewise - the church brotherhood, afterwards - four girls with icons, banner-bearers and the cross-bearer. Under the sounds of bells, the procession goes out of the church and encircles it, beginning from its northern side, and returning from the southern side. When the Priest with the Holy Mysteries reaches the middle of the northern side, the procession stops, interrupts the singing, all kneel, and the Priest blesses all the present people with the Holy Mysteries four times, that is, first directly before himself, then - to the right side, then - behind and finally - to the left, saying each time in a quiet voice: "Save, O God, Thy people and bless Thine inheritance." Similarly it happens on the eastern, southern and western sides[^664]. After the blessing on the western side, the procession enters the church: first the Priest with the Holy Mysteries, other sacred ministers, then - the rest of the procession and the people. Then the Priest places the Holy Mysteries on the holy table, on the folded iliton, and, having laid aside the omophorets, kneels and censes the Holy Mysteries thrice. All kneel, and the Priest, together with other sacred ministers, sings the verses of the supplicatory hymn "Holy God." All the people repeat each of its verses. After the completion of the litiya, the Priest, having censed the Holy Mysteries thrice and having taken upon himself the omophorets, approaches the holy table and, having bowed low, takes the Holy Mysteries and, having turned right to the people, sings piously the verse of the hymn of St. Ambrose "Save Thy people, O Lord" twice, and the people repeat the same. The third time the Priest [sings]: "Save Thy people, O Lord, and bless Thine inheritance"; singing: "and bless," He blesses the people solemnly crosswise, making a half-turn from the right to the left side.
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:12: While the Choir sings the continuation of the Ambrosian hymn, the Priest places the Holy Mysteries on the holy table, goes down before the steps and, having laid aside the omophorets and having censed Them thrice, kneels together with all others until the Deacon hides the Holy Mysteries in the skynia. Then the Great Dismissal takes place, according to custom.
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:23: ##### 2.	The feast of a temple saint, even if of a lower rank, exceeds even a feast of a saint with a Vigil, primarily in that, when it falls on a Sunday, it includes at Matins before the Canon the Prokimenon, the Gospel, and the Sunday Sticheron [after the Gospel], exactly as a Feast of the Theotokos; also, the Gospel Sticheron is transferred from the "Praises" to the end of Matins. Besides this, if it falls on a weekday, it excludes at the Liturgy the Apostle and Gospel of the day, which is then read on the day before the feast. Finally, the temple feast of a saint has its Apodosis, which takes place on the very day of the feast at Vespers for the following day, with an Entrance, as on a Feast of the Lord. In this Apodosis at "Lord, I have cried," there will be 6 stichera: 3 of the temple and 3 of the daily saint; Glory: of the temple; Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday [Octoechos] (Dogmatic) according to the tone of the Doxastikon. If the temple is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, then also Glory, Both now: of the temple. On Friday evening for Saturday, we always sing the Dogmatic of the tone that is being given away. After the Prokimenon - "Let us all say." Aposticha of the temple with its verses, Glory: of the temple, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday [Octoechos], according to the tone of the Doxastikon. If the temple is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, then Both now: of the temple. At the end: Troparion of the temple, Glory: Troparion of the daily saint, Glory, Both now: of the temple. Great Dismissal with the commemoration of the temple and the daily saint. At this Vespers, the service of the temple saint concludes; however, a temple saint does not have an Apodosis if the following day falls on a Sunday, or a Polyeleos saint.
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:262: The general Katavasia of the Theotokos is "I will open," however, on the Sundays of the Prodigal, Meatfare, Cheesefare, on the 1st and 3rd of Lent, on the Saturday of Lazarus, on Palm Sunday and in Passion Week there will be the Katavasia of the Triodion of the Canon of the day. Only during the period of the Katavasia of the Meeting, that is from January 15 to February 9, there will be the Katavasia of the feast, with the exception of Meatfare Sunday, for which the Slavonic rubrics provide the Katavasia of the Triodion (obviously, besides the very day of the feast)[^693].
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:286: ##### 5.	Since the services of the Menaion are diverse, so that the Reader may know what kind of service he should take, to each service on the left side we add one of the signs determining the kind of service.
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:1115: ##### 5.	In the five rows that are between the Publican and the Paschal keys, are indicated all 35 Sundays that may fall between Theophany and the Publican, that is, the Sunday after Theophany and the four last ordinary ones (29th, 30th, 31st and 32nd). However, since not all these Sundays will always take place, the explanation of which we gave on p. 144 [→REF:p144] (for this depends on whether Pascha falls earlier or later), between these five Sundays some places may be unfilled, and this means that then the Sundays corresponding to them, indicated on the right side of the table, will not take place. For example, under the Paschal key "A" all places are unfilled; this means that none of these 5 Sundays will be taken, but immediately after Theophany - the Triodion.
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:1119: ##### 9.	After this, if you want to find out which Sundays in a certain year should be taken between Theophany and the Publican, seek first of all the Paschal key of the following Pascha, using Table I or Table II, and under it in Table III you will find these Sundays; only, if the year is leap, follow immediately the next column. And if you prefer to find out which Sunday, tone and Sunday Gospel are to be taken on one of the Sundays after the Descent of the Holy Spirit, then having determined the Paschal key of this year and having gone down its column to the place and day of the Sunday, follow from it along the transverse row to the right side of the table and you will find all this.
Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md:1125: The curtain, according to the present custom of Athonite monasteries, at the Liturgy, after the completion of the Proskomedia, is drawn open, and at the "Great Entrance" is drawn shut; at "Especially" it is drawn open, and at "And grant that with one mouth" it is drawn shut; at "With fear" it is drawn open and so remains until the conclusion of the Liturgy[^751]. According to the former custom of the Athonite Mountain, when one priest serves, that is without a Deacon, the curtain is drawn shut at the "Great Entrance" and is not drawn open until the end of communion. Besides this, it remains drawn open at Vespers and at Matins from the 1st Hour and at the Moleben. When the Hours are celebrated without Liturgy, it is drawn open for the reading of the Apostle and drawn shut after the dismissal of the "Typika"[^752].
Final_footnotes.md:20: [^17]: Some of us consider that here it is not proper to say "Lord, bless," but only "Bless," because here a blessing is not asked from the Lord, but from the Priest. But this we do not know for certain, for in other Typikons, namely in the Moscow Typikons and Horologia in the order of Vespers, and in the Greek Horologia at Daily Midnight Office, the words "Lord" are absent; however, in our Horologia (at the end of Matins) and in the Tserkovne Oko, and in the Greek Euchologia in the order of Vespers this word is given. Here are the words of the Roman Euchologion: "Ο ιερεύς ησυχως: Δόξα σοι ο θεός, η ελπις ημών, δόξα σοι; και λεγουσιν οι Αδελφοί: Δόξα, και νυν, Κύριε ευλογησον /The Priest quietly: 'Glory to Thee, O God, our hope, glory to Thee.' And the brethren say: 'Glory, Both now,' 'Lord, bless'/." Therefore, both the former and the latter are good, for it is possible to ask the Lord God for a blessing at the conclusion of the Divine Service, just as is asked at Vespers with Vigil, at the beginning of which the Deacon, or the Priest himself, exclaims: Κύριε ευλογησον /"Lord, bless"/.
Final_footnotes.md:44: [^41]: Let it be known that besides the Great Forty Days there exist two other periods in which the rubric of the Menaion on September 21 prescribes also a 3rd Sessional Hymn with a Small Litany and a Kathisma before it. The first period lasts from the Apodosis of the Feast of the Holy Cross, which falls on September 21, to the Forefeast of the Nativity of Christ, which falls on December 20 exclusively. The second lasts from the Apodosis of the Feast of Theophany to Cheese-fare Sunday exclusively, that is in these two periods, in which at Sunday Matins the Polyeleos is given. However, in the content of our Psalter three Sessional Hymns and three Kathismata are given only in the Great Forty Days, so we use them.

Final_footnotes.md:267: [^264]: Ancient Greek typikons mention nothing about a Deacon, neither at the bringing out, nor at the exaltation, nor at the laying of the precious Cross, but only about one priest. The Greek typikon, similarly to the Slavic ones, prescribes for the Priest to vest in all priestly vestments: αλασσει ο Ιερεύς πασαν την ιερατικην στολην /the Priest vests in all priestly vestments/. The Greek Triodion (Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross) and the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, similarly to Slavic typikons, prescribes for the Priest to enter with the cross from the sanctuary to the temple through the northern doors: εκ του αριστερού Βήματος /from the left side of the sanctuary/. Greek typikons, with the exception of the new Constantinopolitan, and the Slavic of the Lviv University, prescribe for the Priest, having exited from the sanctuary to the temple, to head to the entrance doors that lead from the temple to the narthex, which are sometimes called royal, and there await the end of the Doxology and from there exclaim "Wisdom, attend", and then head to the tetrapod, which is before the iconostasis. Although these doors are called royal, βασιλικαι /royal/, one must understand that they were called so in the past. Today we call royal the middle doors of the iconostasis, which according to ancient custom are called holy: αγιαι ΰυραι /holy doors/ (chapter 96, Greek Typikon).
Final_footnotes.md:275: [^272]: Thus our Pochaiv Menaion, Tserkovne Oko and Moscow Typikon. But our liturgikons, horologia and the Greek-Venetian Menaion of 1755 prescribe to take the Service of the Holy Fathers on the Sunday following 11 October; one must understand, that when 11 October falls on a weekday; similarly in the latest Greek-Venetian Menaion (1884) and in the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, where it is prescribed to take on the very day of 11 October, if Sunday, if -- not, then on the following Sunday. What shall we say to this? We consider that all these rules are good, however, it is not good when liturgical books of one and the same church province give uncoordinated rules. For when we accept the rule of our Menaion, then let it be placed also in the liturgikons And in the horologia, for when we accept the rule of the latter, then let It be also in the Menaion. And if we adhere to the rule of the Menaion, then it is necessary to present it already on 8 October, so that, reading, one can notice about the Following Sunday. This must be understood also about the Service of the Holy Fathers of the First Six Councils on 16 July, which the menaia both ours and Greek, Prescribe to transfer back, to 13 July, if it falls on Wednesday; the Greek menaia present this not in accordance with their rule on 11 October, when they prescribe to transfer this service from 16 July back, they have to transfer it back also from 11 October. It seems to us, And it would be simpler not to transfer these services back, but to take on the very [days of] 11 October and 16 July, when they fall on Sunday, if -- not, on the following Sunday.
Final_footnotes.md:280: [^277]: Some consider, according to the edition of the Peremyshl Typikon of 1870, p. 271, that neither on St. Michael, nor on St. Prophet Elias, should "Blessed is the man" be sung and, that then not a single kathisma is taken, for this kathisma, as the publisher writes about it, is found only in the Pochaiv Anthologion, and in the most ancient menaia and Anthologia they are not mentioned; the reason for this is considered to be that "Elias did not die, and St. Michael, as bodiless, is not subject to death". However, this is not enough. For regarding the rule, not only the Pochaiv Anthologion (Anthologion) of 1777 and the Pochaiv Menaion of the same year, but also the Lviv Anthologion (Anthologion) of 1643 and the Great Typikon, under the title Tserkovne Oko, printed in Moscow in 1615, that is more than a century and a half before the Pochaiv Anthologion, and the manuscript Anthologion of 1736, which we have, and the Typikon of the All-Russian Church, printed in Moscow in 1859, by the decision of its Governing Synod, give also on these two feasts the kathisma "Blessed is the man". Although some rules, like the Moscow Menaion of 1782, the Greek-Venetian of 1884 do not give "Blessed is the man", it does not follow from this that no kathisma is needed, for even on Saints without Polyeleos in the Menaion a kathisma is not given, but the sequential one is meant; so here one can understand the sequential one, which three ancient handwritten Slavic typikons give: one -- of the Lviv University, the second -- of St. Onuphrius in Lviv, and the third -- of the library of the Chapter of St. George; also Tserkovne Oko (which gives two rules: one with "Blessed is the man", and the second with the sequential kathisma). The reason that these rules give for these two feasts a sequential kathisma, may lie in the fact that their service initially had neither All-Night Vigil nor Polyeleos, but gradually, with time, grew into All-Night Vigil. About St. Elias it is written in the Pochaiv Menaion: "Let it be known that in printed Greek menaia, in handwritten typikons, and also in Slavic handwritten and printed typikons, the service of the Prophet Elias is given on 6, with Octoechos". And regarding St. Michael, the above-mentioned Slavic typikons give him a service only of the Polyeleos type, but incomplete, for they partially combine his service with the service of the Octoechos, as for a Saint without Polyeleos.

Then the priests approach first, two by two, and kiss the precious Cross, and the Superior signs them with basil with holy water crosswise on the forehead; in the same way also all the brethren. Final_footnotes.md:409: [^406]: About the Katavasia in the odes where the three-ode canon does not come, the typikon is silent. Final_footnotes.md:413: [^410]: Since there are 4 kathismata, that is 3 sequential ones and Polyeleos, the question arises, which litanies should be sung here; for the general rule has only three. To this, since the feast has three sessional hymns, and here only two are given, then which two of the three to sing here? Regarding the litanies, our and Russian typikons mention litanies after the 3rd kathisma, from this it follows that after the 1st and 2nd kathismata there are no litanies, which the Pochaiv Triodion also excludes after the 1st kathisma before the sessional hymn of the Triodion on 25 March; and assuming such, it is proper to take two small litanies: one -- before the post-Polyeleos sessional hymn, with the exclamation "For blessed is Thy name", and the second -- before the sessional hymn of the 3rd kathisma, with the exclamation "For Thou Art good" or "For Thine is the dominion", at choice. But because only the Russian and Romanian typikons do not prescribe the Small Litany at Matins on a weekday, and with us this is not adhered to, therefore we think that here only after the 1st kathisma one need not take a litany, and after the 2nd one should take with the exclamation "For Thine is the dominion", after the 3rd -- with the exclamation "For Thou Art good". And of the two sessional hymns of the feast let one be taken, which is after the Polyeleos, and the second -- one of the first two, at choice.

However, neither in Greek nor in Slavonic rubrics is this met, nor the words of the named psalm fit well here, for according to general explanation they refer not to the descent of Christ into hell, but to His Ascension...

Final_footnotes.md:766: [^763]: These gifts are arranged according to such a scheme so that the blessing of the priest depicts upon them the sign of the cross: loaf • wine • • oil • wheat **Chunk 74** **IV. Rubric of Matins in Sundays and Feasts** **1. In Concelebration of One Deacon** **74.** If before this there was no Vigil, then Matins begins thus^[764]: **75.** The priest puts on the *ryasa* the epitrachelion, having previously blessed it, goes before the holy doors and begins: "Blessed is our God." Choir: "Amen," "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee," "O Heavenly King," Trisagion, "Most Holy Trinity," "Our Father." Priest: "For Thine is the kingdom." Choir: "Amen," "Lord, have mercy" (12), "Glory, Both now," "Come, let us worship" (3). The priest enters the sanctuary through the southern doors, recites the Prayer of the Censer, takes the censer, censes the throne from the four sides, the icon behind the altar and other icons of the sanctuary. Then he goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors, comes before the holy doors and, bowing, censes the icons of the southern part of the iconostasis, beginning from the image of the Savior, the icons of the northern part, beginning from the icon of the Theotokos, then -- the choirs, right and left. He censes the people either from the solea or crossing the whole temple, while the choir reads the 20th(19th) Psalm "The Lord hear thee" and the 21st(20th) "The King shall joy." When the choir begins the Trisagion, the priest returns before the holy doors, censes them and after "Our Father" exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom," enters the sanctuary through the southern doors, stands before the holy table and, after the completion of the troparia "O Lord, save Thy people," exclaims the Litany for Emperors. After the exclamation "For a merciful," Choir: "Amen," "In the name of the Lord, bless, father." The priest exclaims^[765]: "Glory to the Holy, Consubstantial." Choir: "Amen," "Glory to God in the highest" (3) with small bows and "O Lord, open my lips" (2). The Six Psalms are read. **76.** During the reading of the Six Psalms^[766] the priest, standing before the holy table, begins to recite the Matins Prayers. After the first three psalms, he goes out through the northern doors and, having stood before the holy doors, continues to read the Matins Prayers. After the completion of the psalms, the priest responds to the bow of the deacon and, having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, stands before the holy table. At the end of the Six Psalms, the deacon, dressed in the sticharion with the orarion, goes out through the northern doors, pays respect to the priest, stands on his usual place and exclaims the Great Litany. The priest exclaims: "For to Thee belongs." During the singing by the choir of "God is the Lord," the deacon recites the verses and, after a small bow, returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary. **77.** After the Troparia, the reader reads the regular Kathisma, after which the deacon [exclaims] before the holy doors -- the Small Litany, and the priest exclaims: "For Thine is the dominion." Then the reader reads the second regular Kathisma, the deacon -- the second Small Litany, and the priest -- the exclamation "For Thou art a good God." After the Troparia "The Angelic Council," if it is Sunday, or after the Magnification [*Velychannya*], if it is a feast, the deacon again reads the Small Litany, and the priest exclaims: "For blessed." **78.** After the Gradual [*Stepenna*], the holy doors are opened, and the deacon approaches the holy doors and, looking at the people, exclaims: "Let us be attentive." The priest, dressed in the phelonion, blesses the people: "Peace be to all." Deacon: "Wisdom, let us be attentive." The choir sings the Prokimenon. Deacon: "Let us pray to the Lord." Choir: "Lord, have mercy." The priest exclaims: "For Holy art Thou, our God." The deacon censes the holy table from the four sides and everything else, according to custom (see no. 33). Choir: "Let everything that has breath praise the Lord." The deacon recites both verses, and the choir repeats: "Let everything that has breath," after which the deacon: "And that we may be vouchsafed." Choir: "Lord, have mercy" (3). The priest takes the Gospel, goes out before the holy doors, stands at a slight distance before them on the solea, places the Gospel on the analogion and reads it, turned to the people. Deacon: "Wisdom, arise, let us hear the Holy Gospel," and the priest blesses: "Peace be to all." Choir: "And to thy spirit." Priest: "The reading from the Holy Gospel according to (Name)." Deacon: "Let us be attentive." The priest reads the Matins Gospel. **79.** After the reading, the priest kisses the closed book and carries it to the middle of the temple, where he places it on the tetrapod, and he himself returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary. The deacon reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," if it is Sunday, or the reader -- the 50th Psalm "Have mercy on me, O God." After the sticheron, the deacon [is] at his usual place: "O God, save Thy people." Choir: "Lord, have mercy" (12) and the priest exclaims: "By the grace and compassions." Choir: "Amen." The Canon begins. The holy doors, if there was no Vigil, are closed. **80.** If there was a Vigil, the priest after the exclamation "By the grace," holding the cross in his hands, goes out through the holy [doors], and the deacon with the oil, blessed at Vespers, and the anointing brush, -- through the northern doors and they stand on the right side of the tetrapod. There the priest anoints crosswise the forehead of everyone who approaches to kiss the Holy Gospel. After the completion of the anointing [*Myrovanne*] they return to the sanctuary: the priest -- through the holy, and the deacon -- through the southern doors and, having closed the holy doors, the priest takes off the phelonion. **81.** After the third and sixth Odes, the deacon sings the Small Litany either before the holy doors, or near the tetrapod, if the anointing was not yet finished, and the priest exclaims the exclamation either from the sanctuary, or from the tetrapod. After the singing of the Katavasia of the eighth Ode, the deacon takes the censer and, having stood before the icon of the Theotokos, says: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light let us magnify in songs" and, having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, censes the holy table from four sides and everything else, according to the prescription (see no. 33). After the ninth Ode and after the recitation by the choir of "It is truly meet," if it is not Sunday, but if it is Sunday or a feast, after the singing of the Heirmos, the deacon exclaims from his place the Small Litany. The priest exclaims: "For all the powers of heaven praise Thee." The choir sings the Exaposteilarion. **82.** After the Praises stichera, at "Glory," the deacon opens the holy doors. The priest in the phelonion stands before the throne, the deacon stands to his right and, after the completion of the last sticheron, the priest, turned to the east, exclaims: "Glory to Thee, who hast shown us the Light." The Great Doxology is sung. Then, also according to custom, the Troparion is recited, and the priest, preceded by the deacon, carries the Holy Gospel from the tetrapod into the sanctuary and places it on the holy table. **83.** After this, the deacon stands at his usual place and exclaims the litanies "Have mercy on us, O God" and "Let us complete." The priest blesses the people and exclaims: "Peace be to all." Choir: "And to thy spirit." Deacon: "Bow your heads unto the Lord." Choir: "To Thee, O Lord." The priest recites quietly the prayer "O Holy Lord," after which he exclaims: "For Thine it is." The dismissal follows, which the deacon begins: "Wisdom" (see no. 36). **84.** After the dismissal, the reader immediately begins the 1st Hour: "Come, let us worship" (3) and the psalms. After "Our Father," the priest in the epitrachelion: "For Thine is the kingdom." Reader -- Kontakion and "Lord, have mercy" (40). The priest goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors and before the holy [doors], turned to the east, loudly: "Thou who at all times". Reader: "Lord, have mercy" (3), "Glory, Both now", "More honorable than the Cherubim", "In the name of the Lord, bless, father." Priest: "God be merciful to us." Reader: "Amen." Priest recites the prayer "O Christ, the True Light", "Glory to Thee, O Christ God." Reader: "Glory, Both now", "Lord, have mercy" (3), "Bless." The priest imparts the Small Dismissal, returns into the sanctuary and takes off the epitrachelion. **2. In Concelebration of Two Deacons** **85.** At the end of the Six Psalms, the 1st deacon goes out through the northern doors and exclaims the Great Litany, as well as the verses at "God is the Lord" and, having made a small bow, returns into the sanctuary through the southern doors. After the first Kathisma the 2nd deacon, and after the second Kathisma the 1st deacon, sing the Small Litany. After the Troparia "The Angelic Council" or after the Magnifications, the 2nd deacon likewise sings the Small Litany. After the Gradual [*Stepenna*], the 1st deacon: "Let us be attentive." Priest: "Peace be to all." 2nd deacon: "Wisdom, let us be attentive." 1st deacon: "Let us pray to the Lord." Both deacons cense the holy table, the iconostasis, the choirs and the people, as noted in the rubric of Vespers in concelebration with two deacons (see no. 40). At "Let everything that has breath," the 1st deacon recites the first verse, and the 2nd deacon -- the second. **86.** For the anointing of the faithful, the 1st deacon goes out with the priest, and the 2nd exclaims the Small Litany after the third Ode, and also -- after the sixth Ode. After the Katavasia of the eighth Ode, the 1st deacon takes the censer, goes out through the northern doors, stands before the icon of the Theotokos and calls: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," then returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary and, together with the 2nd deacon, censes the throne and everything else, as noted above (see no. 39), and after the censing they return into the sanctuary: the 1st deacon -- through the southern, and the 2nd -- through the northern doors. After the ninth Ode, the 1st deacon exclaims the Small Litany. **87.** After the Praises stichera, the holy doors are opened. The 1st deacon stands before the holy table to the right of the priest, and the 2nd -- to the left. After the Great Doxology, all, having made a small bow, go to the tetrapod, the priest takes the Holy Gospel and, preceded by the deacons, returns into the sanctuary. **88.** After this, the deacons go out: the 1st deacon exclaims the first litany "Have mercy on us, O God," and the 2nd -- the second "Let us complete." 1st deacon: "Bow your heads unto the Lord." The dismissal follows, which the 1st deacon begins: "Wisdom" (see no. 36, note). **3. Without a Deacon** **89.** A priest who serves Matins without a deacon does and says everything that belongs to the deacon, considering this: **90.** After the completion of the Six Psalms, he does not return into the sanctuary, but, standing before the holy doors, exclaims the Great Litany and its exclamation: "For to Thee belongs," as well as the verses during the singing by the choir of "God is the Lord." After the completion of this, he makes a small bow and returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary. It should be noted that when the priest does not have enough time to recite all the Matins Prayers before the iconostasis, he may finish them in the sanctuary during the reading of the Kathismata. After the Prokimenon, he himself exclaims: "Let us pray to the Lord"; reads the verses at "Let everything that has breath"; before the reading of the Gospel, exclaims: "Let us be attentive," and after the Gospel reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ." After the 3rd and 6th Odes of the Canon -- Small Litanies and exclaims from the throne or from the place where he anoints. After the anointing, he returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary and takes off the phelonion. **91.** After the Katavasia of the 8th Ode, he goes out in the epitrachelion through the northern doors, stands before the icon of the Theotokos and calls: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," then censes, as noted above (see no. 33). After the censing, he returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary, exclaims the Small Litany and its exclamation. **92.** After the Praises stichera, at "Glory," he puts on the phelonion, opens the holy doors and, after the last sticheron, exclaims: "Glory to Thee, who hast shown us the Light." Everything else takes place as was presented (see nos. 82-83). **93.** After the dismissal, the priest closes the holy doors, takes off the phelonion and the 1st Hour begins. **4. In Concelebration of Priests** **94.** Concelebrants, in an even number, put on epitrachelia over their *ryasas* and put on phelons during the reading of the Kathisma and stand on the sides of the holy table, as at Vespers (see no. 50). During the singing of the Polyeleos, all go out, having made a small bow, through the holy doors, having lowered the phelonion and hands down, in front -- the younger ones. Two first ones carry the icon of the feast or saint, if it has not yet been placed on the left side of the tetrapod between both choirs, and place it on the tetrapod; all stand on the sides of the tetrapod in two rows, facing one another, the principal celebrant stands in the middle before the tetrapod, and to his right -- the deacon. **
Final_footnotes.md:770: Diagram: At the Magnification** [Left Column] Candle-bearer ... 6 ... 4 ... 2 ... Concelebrants [Center] TETRAPOD [Right Column] Candle-bearer ... 5 ... 3 ... 1 ... Concelebrants [Bottom] Celebrant ... Deacon **95.** Then all sing the Magnification. When the choir repeats the Magnification, the priest, preceded by the deacon with a lighted candle, censes from four sides the icon of the feast or saint on the tetrapod, the holy table, the sanctuary, the iconostasis, the concelebrants, both choirs, the people and once more the icon of the feast, but only from the front. If, however, the usual censing takes place before the Gospel, then at the Magnification the priest censes only the icon itself. Choir: "Glory" and again the Magnification. Then the celebrant and concelebrants sing the Magnification once more. **96.** After the Magnifications all, the younger ones -- in front, return through the holy doors into the sanctuary before the holy table. After the Gospel all, having made a small bow, depart and take off their vestments. **Shortening of Matins**^[767] **1. Beginning** -- The priest, having put on the epitrachelion, censes as usual (see at "Lord, I have cried," no. 75). -- Then he exclaims: "Glory to the Holy," and the choir recites everything else and the 142nd Psalm on Sunday or the regular one -- on feasts. -- After the Great Litany -- "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Sunday or Feast, Theotokion according to the tone of the Troparion. **2. Sung Kathismata** -- Regular Kathismata with Sessional Hymns are not taken, but sung ones are taken thus: "The Angelic Council" -- only on Sundays, "Praise the name of the Lord" (Polyeleos) -- only on feasts, "By the waters of Babylon" -- only on the Sundays of Great Lent. -- The priest at the appointed time puts on the phelonion, opens the holy doors and exclaims: "Peace be to all." Then he censes. If there was censing at the Magnification, then before the Reading of the Gospel there will be no censing. -- Prokimenon, "Let everything that has breath" -- according to the tone of the season. **3. Bringing out the Gospel** -- The priest, having read the Gospel, carries the Holy Book out to the tetrapod, accompanied by two candle-bearers. -- The Reader, if there is no deacon, reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ." -- All go to kiss the Gospel. If there was a *Litiya*, then the anointing takes place (see no. 80). -- Having returned to the throne, the priest recites the prayer "O God, save." After this he does not close the holy doors in view of the shortening of Matins. **4.
