Without a Deacon** **257.** If this Liturgy is celebrated without a deacon, the priest exclaims everything that belongs to the deacon, as has already been said in the rubric of Vespers and the Divine Liturgy of John Chrysostom. **258.** When the priest censes the holy table and transfers the Holy Gifts to the table of oblation, the server, holding a lighted candle, walks before him. Small litanies and exclamations the priest says, if possible, before the holy table. **259.** During the singing of "Let my prayer be set forth" the server walks with a lighted candle before the priest. **260.** At the Great Entrance candle-bearers, if there be such, turned to the northern doors and arranged in order, accompany the Presanctified Lamb. After they arrive before the holy doors they stand together on both sides, and then return to their places. **4. In Concelebration of Priests** **261.** The rules of concelebration of the Liturgy of Presanctified Gifts -- are the same as of Vespers and the Liturgy of Chrysostom. Only one must keep in mind that: -- At the end of the Typika concelebrants vest in sacred vestments, saying when putting on each only: "Let us pray to the Lord." -- The principal celebrant exclaims: "For to Thee belongs," "For a merciful," "According to the gift of Thy Christ," "For Thou art our sanctification." Other exclamations are exclaimed by concelebrants in turn. -- At "Let my prayer be set forth" concelebrants remain in their places, stepping back slightly from the holy table when the principal celebrant needs to pass, together with the principal [celebrant] they bend their knees and rise. -- At the Great Entrance the celebrants carry nothing, however the 1st concelebrant may carry the chalice before the principal celebrant.

The priest kisses the Holy Gospel and places it to the right side on the Holy Table.[278] Then he places the diskarion on the left part of the unfolded eiliton,[279] opens the artophorion, and makes a small bow. He takes the ciborium or pyx in which the Lamb is kept and places it in the center of the eiliton. Then, taking the Presanctified Lamb from the vessel, he places the Lamb upon the diskarion with the greatest reverence; he also takes out the Particles necessary for giving Communion to the faithful.[280] After returning the ciborium or pyx to the artophorion, he closes the artophorion. Then he places the diskarion in the center of the eiliton, censes the asterisk and small veil,[281] and covers the diskarion. Preceded by

Ordo_Celebrationis_1996_CLEAN.md:1746: ##### 243. Priest and deacon leave the Altar via the northern door; the deacon precedes and, walking backwards, censes the Holy Gifts. While so proceeding, they say nothing.[306] After entering the Altar via the Holy Doors, the priest places the diskarion to the left side on the Holy Table, and the chalice to the right; he removes the small veils from the diskarion and the chalice and lays them to one side on the Holy Table. He takes the large veil from the deacon’s shoulder, censes it over the thurible, and with it covers the Holy Gifts, saying nothing, and censes only the Holy Gifts in strokes of three. After the cantors have completed the hymn: Now the powers of heaven ..., all make three great metanies.[307] Then the royal doors and their curtain are closed.[308]

Ordo_Celebrationis_1996_CLEAN.md:2101: The services of a specific feast day or saint’s day are not usually written in one particular tone; at the top of each poetic text the tone number is indicated, and one must know the melody in question by heart and be able to apply it to the words. Sometimes the liturgical books “point” such texts for chanting, but more often they do not. Thus chanters must prepare services with great care.

Ordo_Celebrationis_1996_CLEAN.md:2423: 10) While the Trisagion is being sung by the congregation, it is also sung by the priest who at the same time makes three bows before the Holy Altar. The priest then proceeds to go to the throne behind the altar, the priest saying as he goes: Blessed is he who comes in the name of the Lord. Then he also blessed the throne with the words: Blessed are you on the throne... It is to be noted that the priest does not ascend the throne nor does he sit on it, but he sits on a chair at the left side of the throne. The center chair or throne is always reserved for the bishop.

Ordo_Celebrationis_1996_CLEAN.md:2543: “An altar cannot be considered fit for the celebration of the Divine Liturgy before an iconostas has been erected. Because of the custom of concelebration, it will rarely be advisable to have more than one altar in any church or oratory. If, however, in any edifice of larger dimensions more than one altar is erected for the celebration of the Divine Liturgy, each altar must have its own iconostas.”
